9.0. 112.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference./42/2012) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal- 
cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of ECL 
and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 16/01/2026. 


[No. L-22012/185/2012— IR (CM-ID] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 


ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM- LABOUR COURT, 
ASANSOL. 
PRESENT: Shri Ananda Kumar Mukherjee, 


Presiding Officer, 
C.G.1LT-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 42 OF 2012 


PARTIES: Ganesh Turi 
(dependent son of Late Shanti Turi) 
Vs. 
Management of Jambad OCP, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Milan Kumar Bandyopadhyay, Advocate. 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 26.11.2025 


AWARD 
In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/185/2012-IR(CM-ID) dated 19.11.2012 has been pleased to refer the scheduled dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Jambad OCP under Kajora Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“Whether the action of the management of Jambad OCP in denying employment to the dependent of Late 
Shanti Turi, Ex-Employee is justified? What relief the dependent concerned is entitled to and from 


682 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [Part II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


which date ? ” 


1. On failure of conciliation before the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C), Raniganj at Durgapur, the 
Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi referred the Industrial Dispute bearing Order 
No. L-22012/185/2012-IR(CM-II) dated 19.11.2012 to this Tribunal for adjudication. A Reference case was 
registered on 10.12.2012 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing 
them to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. After service of Notice Ganesh Turi, claiming himself to be the youngest son of Shanti Turi, ex-employee 
filed statement of claim on behalf of the workman. United Koila Mazdoor Sangh (UTUC), the union which raised the 
Industrial Dispute did not take any step. The case of the workman as disclosed in the written statement dated 
04.06.2015 is that Shanti Turi, wife of Late Nibaran Turi was a permanent employee of Eastern Coalfields Limited 
(hereinafter referred to as ECL) and was posted at Jambad OCP under Kajora Area as Haulage Kamin bearing U.M. 
No. 537332. She was appointed in the service of the company on 15.01.1983 and died on 19.11.1994 while in service, 
leaving behind her two sons namely, Haglu Turi and Ganesh Turi. Haglu Turi is physically handicapped. The names 
of the sons appeared in the Service Record Excerpt of Shanti Turi as her dependents and she nominated her sons for 
receiving Coal Mines Provident Fund and Gratuity amount. After death of Shanti Turi, Ganesh Turi submitted an 
application along with relevant documents for his employment on compassionate ground against death of his mother 
as per provisions of National Coal Wage Agreement (hereinafter referred to as NCWA). The grievance of the 
dependent son is that the management did not consider his case for employment and such non-consideration amounts 
to denial of employment which is illegal and unjustified. Ganesh Turi in his written statement has prayed for passing 
an order in his favour, directing management of ECL to provide employment to him at the earliest. 


3. Management of ECL rebutted the claim of the dependent son of the deceased employee by filing a written 
statement on 08.11.2016. It is contended that the terms of reference is misconceived and the claim for employment is 
not in accordance with the provision of NCWA. Management further contended that Ganesh Turi did not submit 
relevant documents in support of his claim that he is the dependent son of the deceased employee or fulfilled all pre- 
requisites under the provisions of NCWA. In the written statement management claim that it is totally justified in not 
providing employment to the dependent of the deceased employee. 


4. The point for adjudication is whether the management of ECL is justified in denying employment to the 
dependent of Late Shanti Turi. 


>: The case was fixed up for evidence since 24.01.2017. Ganesh Turi filed his affidavit-in-chief but did not 
appear for his cross-examination. Several opportunities were granted to the workman for his evidence till 11.06.2024. 
Workman Witness did not turn up for his cross-examination and Mr. Ashish Mohan was examined as Management 
Witness No. 1. He filed his affidavit-in-chief and produced documents which have been marked as Exhibit M-1 to M- 
4. The witness was cross-examined on behalf of Ganesh Turi, claiming to be dependent son of Shanti Turi. 


6. In the affidavit-in-chief Ganesh Turi disclosed that his mother Shanti Turi was a permanent employee of 
ECL at Jambad OCP under Kajora Area of ECL. She died on 19.11.1994 while in the service of the company leaving 
behind him and Haglu Turi, his elder brother as the legal heirs and dependents. Their names are recorded in the 
Service Record Excerpt of their mother. It transpires from his statement that Haglu Turi was handicapped and for 
such reason Ganesh Turi applied for his employment before ECL on consent of his elder brother. The witness claimed 
to have submitted all relevant documents but management did not consider his prayer. The witness did not face the 
cross-examination. Therefore, apart from what is already admitted by the management in their written statements, 
other assertions made in the affidavit-in-chief by Ganesh Turi cannot be considered to have been proved. 


ils Mr. Ashish Mohan, Deputy Manager (Personnel), Jambad OCP has been examined as Management Witness 
No. 1. He filed his affidavit-in-chief relating to the management’s case wherein he admitted that Shanti Turi was an 
employee of ECL who died on 19.11.1994 while in service of the company. It appears from his statement that at first 
Haglu Turi submitted application for employment on 20.03.1997 but as he is physically handicapped such claim was 
not considered by the management. Late Ganesh Turi submitted a separate application on 01.07.2010 for his 
employment i.e., after a long delay. Management witness further stated that as Ganesh Turi was a minor, his claims 
could not be considered by the management and there is no scope to keep his name in the Live Roster of the company 
as per provisions of NCWA. In course of his evidence management witness produced the following documents : 


(i) Copy of the application of Haglu Turi dated 20.03.1997 addressed to the Agent, Jambad OCP, 
claiming employment against the death of his mother is produced as Exhibit M-1. 


(ii) Copy of the Service Record Excerpt of Shanti Turi, as Exhibit M-2. 
(ili) Copy of the Death Registration Certificate of Shanti Turi, as Exhibit M-3 


(iv) Copy of the application of Ganesh Turi addressed to the Agent, Jambad OCP, claiming 
employment, as Exhibit M-4. 


8. In course of cross-examination the management witness admitted that there is a provision for providing 
employment to the physically handicapped person in ECL. He further stated that earlier when the son of Shanti Turi 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 683 


applied for employment, management did not consider the percentage of his disability as he is physically 
handicapped. Apart from application of Ganesh Turi management had no other document to show that any decision 
was taken by the management regarding his prayer for employment. 


9. The case was fixed up on 21.11.2025 for argument but in view of Ministry’s letter No. Z-25025/01/2025- 
CLS-I(E) dated 14.05.2025 to take up old cases for disposal during the special campaign, Notice was issued under 
registered post to both parties, fixing the case for haring of argument. Notice was sent to the General Secretary of 
United Koila Mazdoor Sangh (UTUC) but the same was returned unserved. No Notice was sent to Ganesh Turi at his 
address available in his affidavit-in-chief. 


10. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management of ECL submitted that Haglu Turi who claimed 
employment after the death of his mother by submitting his application dated 20.03.1997 was physically handicapped 
and no employment was provided to him. Thereafter, the younger son Ganesh Turi submitted an application for his 
employment before the Agent of Jambad OCP on 01.07.2010 i.e., sixteen years after the death of his mother. Learned 
advocate argued that at the time of death of Shanti Turi on 19.11.1994 NCWA-V was applicable to the employees and 
management of ECL. To maintain the name of a minor dependent son in the Live Roster his age should have been 
fifteen years or more on the date of death of the employee. In the instant case the prayer for employment of Ganesh 
Turi could not be considered as his age was only eleven years at the time of death of his mother and his name could 
not have been maintained in the Live Roster. The application for employment made in the year 2010 therefore, could 
not be considered and the Industrial Dispute raised against the management is liable to be dismissed. 


11. On the date fixed for hearing of argument none appeared for Ganesh Turi after Notice. I have considered the 
materials on record, pleadings of the parties and the argument advanced on behalf of the management. Undisputedly, 
Haglu Turi and Ganesh Turi are the two dependent sons of Late Shanti Turi and their names appeared in the Service 
Record Excerpt, which is produced as Exhibit M-2. In the year 1987 the age of Ganesh Turi was four (4) years and 
Haglu Turi was thirteen (13) years. Initially, Haglu Turi submitted an application dated 20.03.1997 (Exhibit M-1) 
praying for his employment as a dependent. As he is handicapped, his brother submitted a separate application on 
01.07.2010 (Exhibit M-4), claiming employment for himself. In his application Ganesh Turi disclosed that he had 
submitted an application in the year 2003 for employment but the case file is still pending for consideration. Ganesh 
Turi did not state the exact date of his application made in the year 2003. No such document has come forth for 
consideration. It is noteworthy that by submitting a second claim by Ganesh Turi on 01.07.2010, the claim for 
employment of Haglu Turi was waived. According to Clause 9.5.0 (iii) of NCWA-V which was in force from 
01.07.1991 to 30.06.1996, in case of death of an employee either in mine accident or for other reasons or medical 
unfitness under Clause 9.4.0, if no employment has been offered and the male dependant of the concerned worker is 
fifteen (15) years and above in age he would be kept on a live roster and would be provided with employment 
commensurate with his skill and qualifications when he attains the age of 18 years. During the period the male 
dependant is on live roster, the female dependant will be paid monetary compensation as per rates in Paragraphs (i) & 
(ii). It is crystal clear from the provisions of NCWA that Ganesh Turi was not eligible for employment even on his 
attaining majority as he was only eleven (11) years of age at the time of death of his mother and fell short of four (4) 
years to fulfill the required condition for being considered for employment on being attaining majority. 


12. The provisions of NCWA are strictly followed for the purpose of considering eligibility of a dependent for 
employment. There cannot be any deviation from such rules. In the case of Sukumoni Hembram alias Sukumoni 
Mejhan Vs. Union of India and Others [MAT 27 of 2024] the Hon’ble Division Bench of the Hon'ble High Court 
at Calcutta held : 


“ NCWA is a negotiated agreement that has been arrived at between the employer and the employees. It is a 
settlement under section 2 (p) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 and has binding effect on the parties thereto 
under section 18 (3) of the Act of 1947.” 


13. Under the fact and circumstances, I hold that Ganesh Turi, the dependent son is not entitled to any 
employment under the management of ECL on account of death of his mother in harness. The issue relating to 
employment of Haglu Turi does not arise in this case as neither the union nor Haglu Turi himself appeared before the 
Tribunal to assert his claim. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest. Ganesh Turi, the dependent son of Late Shanti Turi is not 
entitled to get employment under Eastern Coalfields Limited as per provisions of National Coal Wage Agreement — 
V. Let an award be drawn up in light of my above findings. Let copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the 
Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


684 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


नई दिल्‍ली, 27 जनवरी, 2026

सरकार ई सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में

New Delhi, the 27th January, 2026 


S.O. 113.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference./24/2004) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal- 
cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of ECL 
and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 16/01/2026. 


[No. L-22012/459/1998— IR (CM-ID] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 
ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM- LABOUR COURT, 
ASANSOL. 
PRESENT: Shri Ananda Kumar Mukherjee, 
Presiding Officer, 
C.G.I.T-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 24 OF 2004 
PARTIES: Ramashray Ram 
(Dependent son-in-law of Late Bairagi Harijan) 
Vs. 
Management of Dhemomain Colliery, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress. 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 28.11.2025 
AWARD 
In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/459/1998-IR(C-II) dated 28.04.2004 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Dhemomain Colliery under Sodepur Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and 
their workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“Whether the action of the management of Dhemomain Colliery of M/s. ECL in denying employment to Shri 
Ramashray, son-in-law of Shri Bairagi Harijan, ex-employee is legal and justified? If not, to what relief the 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 685 


workman ts entitled? ” 


1. On receiving Order No. L-22012/459/1998-IR(C-II) dated 28.04.2004 from the Government of India, 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case was registered on 19.05.2004 and an 
order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear and submit their 
written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. The union of Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed written statement on 20.08.2004. Fact of the cases which 
unfolds from the written statement is that Bairagi Harijan was posted as Timber Mazdoor at Dhemomain Colliery 
under Eastern Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred to as ECL). He was suffering from illness and the Area 
Medical Board declared him medically unfit for duty on 17.07.1992. The employer company terminated Bairagi 
Harijan from service w.e.f. 23.07.1992 as per provisions of Clause 9.4.0 of National Coal Wage Agreement — IV one 
dependent of the employee who lost his service on account of disablement is entitled to employment under the 
company provided the employee is within fifty-eight (58) years of age and he should have two years of service left. 
At the relevant time Bairagi Harijan had Ramashray Ram his dependent son-in-law and nominated him for 
employment. He also submitted his application and relevant documents for employment of his son-in-law. 
Management held screening of documents and forwarded the proposal for employment to ECL Headquarters through 
Area Office. The management verified the relationship between Ramashray Ram and Bairagi Harijan through Police 
from his native place and the same was confirmed. It is contended that in violation of provisions of National Coal 
Wage Agreement (hereinafter referred to as NCWA) management did not provide any employment to the dependent 
son-in-law. According to the union the act of the management is unjust and necessary direction should be given to the 
employer company to provide employment to Ramashray Ram without further delay. 


3. After several opportunities management failed to file any written statement until 08.05.2023, for nineteen 
years. The specific case of the management as per their written statement is that Bairagi Harijan, an employee of ECL 
at Dhemomain Colliery was declared medically unfit by the Area Medical Board and his service was terminated by 
the management on 23.07.1992. The ex-employee nominated his son-in-law to provide employment in his place as 
per provisions of NCWA. As the relationship of alleged son-in-law and the ex-workman was not sufficiently 
established, the claim for employment was not considered by the management. According to the management the 
relationship between the alleged son-in-law and the workman was doubtful. Therefore, management was unable to 
provide employment. It is claimed that the decision of the management is proper and the nominated person is not 
entitled to any relief. 


4. The only question for consideration is whether management of Dhemomain Colliery is justified in denying 
employment to Ramashray Ram, claiming to be son-in-law of Bairagi Harijan. 


5. During pendency of the case Bairagi Harijan died on 14.11.2014. In the instant case Ramashray Ram has 
been examined as Workman Witness No. |. He filed an affidavit-in-chief where from it appears that in September 
2023, he was 49 years of age. The witness stated that he is the son-in-law of Bairagi Harijan. His father-in-law was 
examined by Medical Board constituted by the company and was declared medically unfit on 17.07.1992. The 
company terminated the service of Bairagi Harijan on 23.07.1992. The witness further deposed that his father-in-law 
submitted an application claiming employment for him as son-in-law and also submitted relevant documents. The 
employment proposal was processed and after proper screening at Colliery and Area Level it was forwarded to the 
ECL Headquarters. The management verified the relationship between Bairagi Harijan and Ramashray Ram, seeking 
verification through Police authority, who confirmed that Ramashray Ram is the son-in-law of Bairagi Harijan. The 
management thereafter did not provide employment to the dependent son-in-law. It is stated that he has no means of 
livelihood and requires employment. The witness further stated that he was wholly dependent on the earing of Bairagi 
Harijan, who died on 14.11.2014, leaving behind his daughter Smt. Bechni Debi and Ramashray Ram, the son-in-law. 
Witness further stated that he was residing in the house of his father-in-law during his lifetime and now at his native 
village with other family members. It is stated that Bairagi Harijan had no son and Bechni Devi is the only daughter 
who has been married to him during the lifetime of his father-in-law. In course of his examination-in-chief the 
workman witness produced the following documents in support of his case : 


(i) Photocopy of Order dated 20/23.07.1992 whereby Bairagi Harijan was declared medically unfit is 
produced as Exhibit W-1. 


(ii) Photocopy of the application submitted by Bairagi Harijan on 25.03.1993 for employment of 
Ramashray Ram, as Exhibit W-2. 


(iii) Photocopy of the letter dated 01.12.1993 which was issued by the Dy. CME/Agent, Dhemomain 
Colliery to Ramashray Ram for appearing before the Screening Committee, as Exhibit W-3. 


(iv) Photocopy of another letter whereby Ramashray Ram was asked to appear before the Screening 
Committee once again on 22.01.1994, as Exhibit W-4. 


(v) Photocopy of the Marriage Certificate of Ramashray Ram issued by the Marriage Officer, Asansol, 
as Exhibit W-5. 


(vi) Photocopy of Police Report dated 06.11.1995 issued by the Superintendent of Police, Azamgarh, as 


686 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [Part II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Exhibit W-6. 


(vii) Photocopy of the Office Order dated 22.08.1996 whereby Ramashray Ram was asked to appear 
before the screening committee at Area Office on 24.08.1996, as Exhibit W-7. 


(Vili) Photocopy of the Letter dated 08/11.09.1995 whereby the union representative was informed that 
the prayer of Ramashray Ram would be considered after receiving Police Verification Report, as 
Exhibit W-8. 


6. In cross-examination the witness deposed that he got married to Bechni Debi on 23.05.1992 at Ramgarh 
(Azamgarh). It transpires from his cross-examination that his marriage was registered for the second time in the year 
1996 and a copy of Marriage Certificate was produced as Exhibit W-9 as he was asked to produce Marriage 
Certificate for the second time. In cross-examination Ramashray Ram stated that he was married before his father-in- 
law was declared medically unfit. He further deposed that he continued to reside with his father-in-law at Dhemomain 
Colliery but he is not a Voter of Dhemomain. Witness denied the suggestion that he is not the son-in-law of Bairagi 
Harijan or he is not the dependent of Bairagi Harijan. 


7. Management produced no evidence in this case. 


8. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress argued that management admitted the fact that 
Bairagi Harijan was an employee of ECL until he was declared medically unfit and terminated from his service. It is 
also established from the evidence on record that an application was submitted by the ex-employee on 25.03.1993 for 
employment of Ramashray Ram, his son-in-law which has been marked as Exhibit W-2. The management on the 
basis of such application asked Ramashray Ram to appear before the Screening Committee on 04.12.1993 by issuing 
letter dated 01.12.1993 (Exhibit W-3). He was again directed to appear before the Screening Committee on 
22.01.1994 (Exhibit W-4) and for the third time on 24.08.1996 by letter dated 22.08.1996 (Exhibit W-7). Mr. Kumar 
submitted that the management verified the relationship between the ex-employee and Ramashray Ram and the 
Superintendent of Police, Azamgarh, Uttar Pradesh by letter dated 06.11.1995 addressed to the Agent, Dhemomain 
Colliery (Exhibit W-6) informed that Ramashray Ram is the son-in-law of Bairagi Harijan, but management neither 
allowed the proposal for employment nor regret the claim. Mr. Kumar submitted that according to the provisions of 
NCWA the dependent son-in-law who has been nominated by the employee should be provided with employment. 


9. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management of ECL argued that Ramashray Ram in his affidavit-in- 
chief has stated that he married Bechni Debi, the daughter of Bairagi Harijan on 23.05.1992 but in the Certificate of 
Marriage (Exhibit W-5) it is stated that the marriage took place on 187 day of March, 1993. Learned advocate for the 
management argued that this Marriage Certificate has been prepared only for making a claim for employment after 
the ex-employee was terminated from his service on 23.07.1992. It is further argued that the union has failed to 
prove that Ramashray Ram is the dependent son-in-law of the ex-employee, Bairagi Harijan. Referring to 
Exhibit W-6, Mr. Das submitted that the Police at Azamgarh after holding enquiry submitted a report to the Agent of 
Dhemomain Colliery dated 06.11.1995 only informing that there was no adverse report against Ramashray Ram, son 
of Sabhuti Ram, Vill- Rampur Katharawa, PO- Devgaon, Dist- Azamgarh and the report does not establish the 
relationship between Bairagi Harijan and Ramashray Ram as father-in-law and son-in-law. It is strenuously argued 
that the alleged son-in-law could not establish his relationship with the ex-employee. The management is not in a 
position to provide employment to Ramashray Ram and urged that the Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


10. I have carefully considered the pleadings of the parties, evidence adduced by the workman witness, 
documents produced and the argument advanced on behalf of the management and union in support of their 
respective cases. The material on record and the admitted position of this case makes it abundantly clear that Bairagi 
Harijan, the ex-employee of ECL was posted at Dhemomain Colliery and terminated from his service on 23.07.1992 
on the ground of his being declared medically unfit. It is also admitted that as per the provisions of Clause 9.4.0 of 
NCWA-V a dependent of the employee who is declared medically unfit, is entitled to employment under the 
employer company provided that the concerned employee was below fifty-eight years of age. In the instant case no 
dispute has been raised by the management that the ex-employee was above the age of fifty-eight (58) years or that 
the dependent of such employee is not entitled to any employment. In the present case the concerned workman 
submitted an application on 25.03.1993 addressed to the Welfare Officer of Dnemomain Colliery for employment of 
one Ramashray Ram, describing him as his son-in-law (Exhibit W-2). Clause 9.4.0 (iii) of NCWA-V provides that for 
the purpose of being considered as dependent, wife / husband as the case may be, unmarried daughter, son and legally 
adopted son may be considered for employment but if no such direct dependant is available for employment younger 
brother, widowed daughter / widowed daughter-in-law or son-in-law residing with the employee and almost wholly 
dependent on the earning of the employees may be considered for employment. From the evidence on record, it 
appears that Ramashray Ram was directed to appear before the Screening Committee on three occasions by issuing 
letters which have been marked as Exhibit W-3, W-4 and W-7. Management called for Police report from Azamgarh, 
Uttar Pradesh to find out if there was any relationship between Bairagi Harijan and Ramashray Ram. A letter dated 
06.11.1995 was issued by the Superintendent of Police, Azamgarh which is absolutely silent about the relationship 
between Ramashray Ram and Bairagi Harijan. It is only mentioned that there was no adverse report regarding 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 687 


character and antecedents of Ramashray Ram. At the time of hearing this case Bairagi Harijan had expired. 
Ramashray Ram did not examine Bechni Debi, the daughter of Late Bairagi Harijan to support his case. In his cross - 
examination the workman witness deposed that he got married to Bechni Debi, the daughter of Bairagi Harijan on 
23.05.1992 and he produced the Marriage Certificate dated 16.01.1996/20.02.1996 only because he was asked to 
submit Marriage Certificate. On a perusal of Marriage Certificate (Exhibit W-5), I find that the marriage under the 
Special Marriage Act, 1954 took place and was registered on 18.03.1993, which was much after the termination of 
service of Bairagi Harijan on 23.07.1992. If the subsequent marriage registration under the Hindu Marriage Act, 1955 
dated 20.02.1996 is considered, it would appear that the alleged Hindu Marriage took place on 23.05.1992 (Exhibit 
W-9), that is only two months before the date of termination. The date of marriage in the two documents apparently 
contradict each other, raising doubt. The daughter of Bairagi Harijan was not examined as a witness to set this issue at 
rest and establish their relationship. Therefore, it can safely be held that there was no marriage prior to declaring 
Bairagi Harijan medically unfit. There is no evidence on record to establish that a relationship of son-in-law and 
father-in-law subsisted between Ramashray Ram and Bairagi Harijan. Accordingly, the management of ECL has 
committed no illegality in not processing the claim for employment of Ramashray Ram as a dependent of Bairagi 
Harijan. I find no merit in the case and the same is dismissed on contest. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest against the union. The action of the management of 
Dhemomain Colliery under Sodepur Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited in not providing employment to Ramashray 
Ram as dependent son-in-law of Bairagi Harijan calls for no interference. Let an award be drawn up in light of my 
above findings. Let copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New 
Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 27 जनवरी, 2026

New Delhi, the 27th January, 2026 


S.O. 114.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference./02/2023) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal- 
cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of ECL 
and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 16/01/2026. 


[No. L-22012/104/2022— IR (CM-ID)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 


ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM- LABOUR COURT, 
ASANSOL 
PRESENT: Shri Ananda Kumar Mukherjee, 


Presiding Officer, 
C.G.I.T-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 02 OF 2023 
PARTIES: Manoj Kumar Nunia 
Vs 


688 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


Management of Mouthdih Unit, Sodepur (R) Colliery, Sodepur Area, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 24/11/2025 

AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/104/2022-IR(CM-IID) dated 19/12/2022 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Mouthdih Unit, Sodepur (R) Colliery, Sodepur Area of Eastern Coalfields 
Limited and their workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“Whether the action of the Management of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Ltd. in relation to its Mouthdih Unit, 
Sodepur (R) Colliery under Sodepur Area in dismissing the service of Shri Manoj Kumar Nunia, UG Loader, 
U.M. No. 102596 vide their letter No. PD/C-6/17/36/155/001876 dated 05-14/10/2017 is fair, legal and 
Justified? If not, to what relief the concerned workman is entitled to?” 


1. On receiving Order No. L-22012/104/2022-IR(CM-II) dated 19/12/2022 from the Government of India, 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 02 of 2023 was registered on 
05/01/2023 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear 
and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. General Secretary, Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed written statement for Manoj Kumar Nunia, ex-employee 
of ECL on 02/05/2023. In nutshell, facts of the case as disclosed in the written statement is that Manoj Kumar Nunia 
was a permanent employee of ECL and was posted as an Underground Loader at Mouthdih Unit, Sodepur (R) 
Colliery, ECL. Workman was absent from duty due to his serious illness which was beyond his control and he was 
unable to perform the duty from 28/11/2015 to 20/04/2016 i.e. a period for less than five months. During his absence 
from duty, he underwent medical treatment. When Manoj Kumar Nunia reported for duty, management of the colliery 
issued charge sheet against him bearing No. SC/C-6/F-07/2016/0870 dated 21/25.04.2016. Management thereafter 
held departmental enquiry against the workman and finally issued an order of dismissal bearing No. PD/C- 
6/17/36/155/001876 dated 05/14.10.2017, dismissing the workman from service. Dismissed workman thereafter 
submitted mercy appeal and prayed for allowing him to join duty. According to the union, absence of Manoj Kumar 
Nunia from duty was beyond his control and he was not given ample opportunity to defend himself. Enquiry Officer 
did not consider medical certificates submitted by the workman during enquiry proceeding. Furthermore, charged 
employee was not allowed to avail assistance of co-workers during enquiry. Signature of the workman was obtained 
on documents and on conclusion of enquiry, workman was found guilty of misconduct. Taking advantage of illiteracy 
of the workman and without considering the treatment papers, Enquiry Officer held the enquiry in violation of the 
principle of Natural Justice. It is prayed that workman may be reinstated in service and back wages paid to him for the 
period of his unintentional absence from duty and the period of his termination. 


3 Management of ECL contested the reference by filing written statement on 21/03/2023 where it is disclosed 
that Manoj Kumar Nunia was absent from duty from 28/11/2015 to 20/04/2016 for which charge sheet was issued 
against him on 21/04/2016 under clauses 26.29, 26.23, 26.05 and 26.22 i.e. for unauthorized absence from duty 
beyond ten days, habitual absence from duty without sufficient cause, willful neglect of work and sabotage or causing 
willful damage to work in progress or to property of the company respectively. Mr. Ravi Bara, Asst. Manager (Per) 
was appointed as the Enquiry Officer for holding enquiry in respect of the charges levelled against Manoj Kumar 
Nunia. Enquiry Officer provided opportunity to the workman to defend his case, holding enquiry on 05/05/2017, 
09/05/2017 and 16/05/2017. Workman participated in the enquiry and defended himself. Enquiry Officer observed the 
principle of Natural Justice during holding the enquiry. From office record, it would appear that on previous three 
years, attendance of the workman was poor. In the year 2013, the workman attended duty for only twelve days, in the 
year 2014 his attendance was NIL and in the year 2016 until issuance of charge sheet he attended duty for six days 
only. After completion of the enquiry proceeding, report was submitted to the Disciplinary Authority. The 
Disciplinary Authority issued a second show cause Notice bearing No. PD/C-6/17/Absenteeism/3/788 dated 
09/06/2017 which was sent to the concerned workman through registered post. Workman did not reply to the second 
show cause Notice and he was finally dismissed from service by the Disciplinary Authority. Management contended 
that the quantum of punishment passed against the workman is proportionate to the nature of misconduct and he is not 
entitled to any relief. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 689 


4. Short point for consideration is whether action taken by the management against Manoj Kumar Nunia in 
dismissing him from service of the company is legal and justified? If not, what relief the workman is entitled to? 


5. Both parties adduced evidence in this case. Manoj Kumar Nunia filed an affidavit-in-chief where he 
reiterated his case disclosed in the written statement. Objection raised by the workman is that he was absent due to his 
illness which was beyond his control and enquiry was held without providing him opportunity to defend his case as 
medical certificates were ignored by the Enquiry Officer. He further stated that he had also submitted death certificate 
of his sister which was one of the reasons for his absence from duty. Manoj Kumar Nunia examined himself as 
Workman Witness-I and he produced the following documents: 


(i) copy of charge sheet issued to the workman due to his absence is marked as Exhibit W-1. 
(ii) copy of enquiry proceeding in five pages is collectively marked as Exhibit W-2. 


(iii) copy of application dated 09/12/2019 whereby the workman along with his family members had prayed for 
allowing him to join duty is marked as Exhibit W-3. 


(iv) copy of another application submitted by the workman along with his family members requesting the 
management to allow him to join duty is marked as Exhibit W-4. 


(५) copy of application dated 04/04/2022 for allowing the workman to join duty is marked as Exhibit W-5. 


During cross-examination workman witness admitted that he received second show cause Notice at his home 
address before his dismissal from service. Workman further admitted that in the year 2015 he attended duty for only 
six days, in the year 2014 he was absent throughout the year and in the year 2013 he attended duty for twelve days 
only. Witness deposed that he informed that Enquiry Officer that due to illness of his sister he was unable to attend 
duty. It transpires from the testimony of workman witness that he did not submit application informing the 
management about his illness nor did he take any prior permission for his absence from duty. 


6. Mr. Sumanta Bhattacharya is examined as Management Witness-I. He filed an affidavit-in-chief reaffirming 
the statements made in written statement of the management. Witness further stated that since misconduct of the 
workman was serious and grave in nature, management decided to dismiss the workman from service. Management 
issued a second show cause Notice to the workman but he failed to submit reply against findings of the Enquiry 
Officer. Witness further averred that management is justified in dismissing the workman from service and he is not 
entitled to any relief of reinstatement or back wages. In course of examination, witness produced the following 
documents: 


(i) Copy of charge sheet dated 21/04/2016 by which charges under clause 26.29 for absenting from duties 
beyond ten days without sanction leave, under clause 26.5 for willful neglect of work, under clause 26.22 for 
sabotage or causing willful damage to work in progress or to property of the company and under cause 26.23 
for habitual late attendance or habitual absence from duty without sufficient cause were levelled against him 
is marked as Exhibit M-1 


(ii) Copy of office order dated 20/21.05.2016 by which Ravi Bara, Asst. Manager (P) was appointed as the 
Enquiry Officer is marked as Exhibit M-2. 


(iii) Copy of application submitted by Manoj Kumar Nunia seeking time for attending the enquiry proceeding is 
marked as Exhibit M-3. 


(iv) Copy of enquiry proceeding in four pages is collectively marked as Exhibit M-4. 
(५) Copy of enquiry report dated 01/06/2017 is marked as Exhibit M-5. 
(vi) Copy of second show cause Notice is marked as Exhibit M-6. 


(vii) Copy of order of dismissal dated 05/14.10.2017 issued by the General Manager, Sodepur Area, whereby he 
was dismissed from service for his misconduct is marked as Exhibit M-7. 


(viii) | Copy of Note sheet dated 02/03.06.2017 proposing termination of service of the workman in two pages is 
collectively marked as Exhibit M-8. 


In cross-examination Management witness deposed that the Enquiry Officer extended opportunity to the 
charged workman for engaging co-workers to assist him in the enquiry. He stated that after dismissal from service, 
workman submitted three mercy petitions praying for his reinstatement in his service. Dismissed workman was not 
informed about finality of mercy petitions submitted by him. He also denied suggestion that punishment of dismissal 
from service of the workman was disproportionate to the nature of misconduct. 


7. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative, arguing the case on behalf of the dismissed workman submitted 
that the alleged unauthorized absence of the workman was for a period of four months and twenty two days only. The 
Workman participated in the enquiry proceeding and produced documents in support of his illness but the 


690 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SECc. 3(ii)] 


management without providing proper opportunity to represent his case dismissed the workman from service. Union 
representative argued that nature of punishment is also disproportionate to the nature of misconduct mentioned in the 
charge sheet, which is absence for less than five months. Union representative accordingly prayed for setting aside the 
order of dismissal passed against Manoj Kumar Nunia and for his reinstatement in service along with back wages. 


8. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate in reply argued that the workman had burdened the management by his 
habitual absence from duty and his attendance in the previous three years was poor. Workman was given several 
opportunities but he did not amend his conduct and continued to remain absent without any information and seeking 
prior permission from the management. He submitted that contradictory reasons were cited by the workman for his 
unauthorized absence from duty. After receiving charge sheet, he submitted an application dated 21/03/2017 
addressed to the Asst. Manager (P) seeking time for enquiry on the ground that due to physical problem he could not 
attend enquiry on 23/08/2016. During his participation in the enquiry, no document was submitted to prove that he 
was suffering from illness. Ld. Advocate strenuously argued that workman willfully neglected his duty in a habitual 
manner and the management found him guilty of charge under clauses 26.23 and 26.29 of the Certified Standing 
Order. All possible measures were taken and the principle of Natural Justice was followed at the time of enquiry. The 
Disciplinary Authority issued a second show cause Notice to the workman dated 09/14.06.2017 (Exhibit M-6) 
seeking his explanation or reply within fifteen days. Workman initially denied that he received second show cause 
Notice but, in the cross-examination admitted that he received second show cause Notice. No reply was submitted by 
the workman to deny the findings against him. Management did not find extenuating circumstances in the conduct of 
the workman to take a liberal view or impose a lighter punishment. It is argued that decision taken by the 
management is appropriate and the workman is not entitled to any relief. 


9. I have considered the argument advanced on behalf of the workman and the management in light of the 
Industrial Dispute raised, attending facts and circumstances of the case derived from pleading and evidence on record. 
Workman was admittedly absent from 28/11/2015 to 20/04/2016. He did not inform the management that he was 
unable to attend duty for any plausible reason nor did he take prior permission from the employer company. 
Management therefore was in dark about attendance of the workman. After a passage of more than four months of 
absence, a charge sheet was issued to him disclosing the charges of unauthorized absence from duty beyond ten days, 
habitual absence from duty without sufficient cause, willful neglect of work and sabotage or causing willful damage 
to work in progress or to property of the company. Workman did not care to submit reply to the charge sheet. He 
turned up on 21/03/2017 and sought for time to attend the enquiry. Enquiry Officer extended reasonable opportunity 
to the charged workman to participate in the proceeding and defend himself against the arraignment. Statement of 
Manoj Kumar Nunia was recorded after the management witnesses were examined in support of the charge. During 
enquiry, workman stated that he absented from duty due to his sister’s illness who was admitted in Mission Hospital, 
Durgapur, with some incurable disease and she died on 25/05/2016. He went further stating before the Enquiry officer 
that he was mentally disturbed for problems in his family. In cross-examination, charged employee was asked 
whether he informed the Unit Management about his absence or illness to which he replied in the negative. He was 
categorically questioned if he can show prescription made by the Doctor to which the workman also answered in the 
negative. From the materials in enquiry proceeding, it appears that the workman never claimed to be suffering from 
illness nor did he produce medical documents before the Enquiry officer. I find from the record that the Enquiry 
Officer in course of the enquiry proceeding observed the principle of Natural Justice and arrived at a finding on the 
basis of statements made before him. Claim by the workman in written statement that he was suffering from illness 
and produced medical documents are outcome of afterthought. Cross-examination of WW-I reveals that his past 
attendance was extremely poor. Therefore, the findings of the Enquiry Officer against the workman appears consistent 
with the fact and circumstances which have been established against the workman through evidence. Enquiry Officer 
in his finding dated 01/06/2017 (Exhibit M-5) observed that Manoj Kumar Nunia failed to produce medical 
prescription or cash memo of purchase of medicines during the period of his absence. The Enquiry Officer concluded 
that charge levelled under clauses 26.23 and 26.29 of Certified Standing Order stood proved beyond doubt. General 
Manager, Sodepur Area in a fair manner provided second opportunity to the workman for his explanation regarding 
finding made against him. Second show cause Notice dated 09/14.06.2017 has been produced as Exhibit M-6. 
Workman in a nonchalant manner during his examination denied receiving second show cause Notice for gaining 
some sympathy in his favour. However, contrary to his earlier statement admitted in the cross-examination that 
second show cause Notice was served upon him but the workman did not reply to the same. Therefore, the 
Disciplinary Authority after considering all materials and previous attendance of the workman dismissed him from 
service. Order of dismissal was communicated to Manoj Kumar Nunia under registered post with AD (Exhibit M-7). 
It is true that dismissed workman thereafter made attempts to regain his employment by submitting mercy petitions on 
09/12/2019, 04/03/2020 and 04/04/2022 (Exhibits W-3, W-4 and W-5 respectively). Even this approach of the 
workman lacked promptitude as he came up with mercy petitions after two years from his dismissal on 14/10/2017. In 
my considered view, charge has been well established against the workman through a proper enquiry. There is no 
mitigating circumstance in favour of the workman for his reinstatement in service by casting aside his continuous 
misconduct of unauthorized and habitual absence. Punishment awarded against the workman arising out of domestic 
enquiry appears to be just, proportionate and legally tenable. The dismissed workman is not entitled to any relief in 
this case and the same is dismissed. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 691 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest against the workman. The dismissed workman is not entitled to any 
relief of reinstatement in service or back wages. Management of ECL is directed to make payment of any legal dues 
to the workman on account of his past service. Let an Award be drawn up in the light of my above discussion. Let 
copies of Award be communicated to the Ministry for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 27 जनवरी, 2026 


का.आ. 115.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ई सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजको और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण-सह-श्रम न्यायालय, आसनसोल के पंचाट[संदर्भ संक्या -11/2013) को vaya 
करती है, जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को 16/01/2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था। 


[सं. एल-22012/102/2013-आई.आर. (सीएम-1)] 


सलोनी, उप निदेशक/लिंक अधिकारी 
New Delhi, the 27th January, 2026 


S.O. 115.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference./11/2013) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal- 
cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of ECL 
and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 16/01/2026. 


[No. L-22012/102/2013— IR (CM-ID] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 


ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM- LABOUR COURT, 
ASANSOL 
PRESENT: Shri Ananda Kumar Mukherjee, 


Presiding Officer, 
C.G.1LT-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 11 OF 2013 


PARTIES: Chhatrapati Majhi 
Vs 

Management of Ghusick Colliery, Kalipahari (R) under Sripur Area, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 27/11/2025 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/102/2013-IR(CM-II) dated 21/08/2013 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Ghusick Colliery, Kalipahari (R) under Sripur Area of Eastern Coalfields 
Limited and their workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“Whether the action of the management of Ghusick Colliery Kalipahari (R) under Sripur Area to dismiss 


692 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


from service to Sri Chhatrapati Majhi of Ghusick Colliery under Kalipahari (R) is fair, justified and 
proportionate punishment for absence from duty? If not so, what relief management can provide to him?” 


1. On receiving Order No. L-22012/102/2013-IR(CM-II) dated 21/08/2013 from the Government of India, 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 11 of 2013 was registered on 
14/02/2014 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear 
and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed written statement for the dismissed workman 
on 05/08/2014. In brief, fact of the case disclosed in the written statement of the union is that Chhatrapati Majhi was a 
permanent employee of ECL and was posted at Ghusick Colliery unit of Kalipahari (R) colliery under Sripur Area of 
ECL. He joined service on 26/10/1981 having U. Man No. 120909. Due to his illness, Chhatrapati Majhi could not 
attend his duty under ECL. After recovery from illness, he reported for joining duty but was neither allowed to join 
his duty nor did the management issue any order of dismissal against him. Workman waited for a long period and 
thereafter requested the union to take steps for his joining duty. Workman submitted a mercy petition before the 
management which was not considered. Thereafter, he raised an Industrial Dispute which ended in failure and the 
Ministry of Labour, Government of India referred the scheduled dispute before this Tribunal for adjudication. 
According to the workman, management should have issued charge sheet and should have conducted an enquiry as 
per the Certified Standing Order of the company. After holding enquiry, a decision could have been taken either to 
allow him to join duty or to punish him but the management without complying the essential prerequisites did not 
allow the workman to join his duty. According to the union, Chhatrapati Majhi has no employment and is unable to 
maintain his family. Union claimed that there is no fault on the part of Chhatrapati Majhi and he was forced to seat 
idle for the entire period. Union prayed for reinstatement of the workman in service along with full back wages and 
other consequential benefits. 


3. Management of Kalipahari Group of Mines, Sripur Area, ECL filed their written statement on 16/10/2023 
wherein it is stated that the instant dispute has been raised by the union over dismissal of Chhatrapati Majhi. 
According to the management, the ex-workman was charge sheeted for his unauthorized absence from duty but the 
workman failed to submit satisfactory reply to the charge sheet due to which a domestic enquiry was conducted by the 
Enquiry officer. Workman duly participated in the enquiry proceeding and he was given full opportunity to defend his 
case according to the principle of Natural Justice. The Enquiry Officer found Chhatrapati Majhi guilty of charge and 
submitted his enquiry report, enquiry proceeding and other relevant documents. Workman was dismissed from service 
on being found guilty of misconduct for unauthorized absence from duty. Management denied that enquiry 
proceeding was vitiated due to irregularities or that the punishment awarded to Chhatrapati Majhi was 
disproportionate. Management denied that the workman was unable to attend duty due to his illness. It is urged that 
fairness of enquiry is required to be decided as a preliminary issue and if the Tribunal found that the enquiry 
proceeding was unfair for any reason, management may be granted opportunity to prove the charge established 
against the workman by adducing further evidence. It is claimed that the action of the management in dismissing the 
workman is fully justified and he is not entitled to any relief. 


4. Short point for consideration in this reference is whether dismissal of Chhatrapati Majhi from service is fair 
and justified and if punishment awarded is proportionate to the misconduct of absence from duty? If not, what relief 
the dismissed workman is entitled to? 


5. After extending several opportunities management did not file any written statement and the case was fixed 
for evidence of workman witness on 26/09/2016. No evidence was adduced by the workman. On prayer of ECL, a 
belated written statement was filed after service of copy upon the union representative. Union did not adduce any 
evidence in favour of the workman witness. Finally, one Mr. Apurba Biswas was examined as Management Witness- 
I. In his affidavit-in-chief management witness, who was posted as Manager (P) at Kalipahari (R) Colliery, ECL 
stated that Chhatrapati Majhi was absent from duty from 11/07/1983 and was charge sheeted on 09/11/1983 under 
section 29(16) of Certified Standing Order of the company. Workman submitted reply to the charge sheet which was 
found unsatisfactory and a domestic enquiry was held in respect of the chargelevelled against the workman. Enquiry 
Officer issued Notice of Enquiry and after holding enquiry submitted his report. Workman did not participate in the 
enquiry proceeding to defend his case and the charge of misconduct was established against the workman. The 
Disciplinary Authority after careful consideration of the charge sheet, enquiry proceeding, enquiry report and all other 
connected papers issued order of dismissal of Chhatrapati Majhi from service vide letter dated 04/08.06.1984. 
According to the management, punishment was proportionate to the gravity of misconduct committed by the 
workman. Witness denied that the workman was prevented from his duty due to his illness. It is claimed that no 
intimation was given by the workman to the management for such unauthorized absence. Copies of enquiry 
proceeding and finding have been admitted in evidence. Following documents have been produced by the 
Management Witness-I: 


(i) Copy of charge sheet dated 09/11/1983 issued against Chhatrapati Majhi is marked as Exhibit M-1. 


(ii) Copy of reply submitted by the workman against the charge sheet, which appears as an explanatory note on 
the charge sheet is marked as Exhibit M-1/1. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 693 


(iii) Copy of letter of appointment of the Enquiry Officer dated 02/03/1984 is marked as Exhibit M-2. 


(iv) Copy of Notice of Enquiry dated 04/03/1984 which was issued by the Enquiry Officer and sent to 
Chhatrapati Majhi under registered post is marked as Exhibit M-3. 


(५) Xerox copy of postal envelope in which the Notice of Enquiry dated 04/03/1984 was sent to Chhatrapati 
Majhi at his address at Vill- Bhatta Para (Ningah) is marked as Exhibit M-4. 


(vi) Copy of enquiry proceeding is collectively marked as Exhibit M-5 and copy of finding of the enquiry is 
marked as Exhibit M-5/1. 


(vii) | Copy of letter dated 04/08.06.1984 issued by the General Manager addressed to Chhatrapati Majhi informing 
him that he has been dismissed from service with immediate effect is marked as Exhibit M-6. 


(viii) | Copy of letter issued by the management in respect of an application under RTI informing that Chhatrapati 
Majhi’s representation for reinstatement was not found fit for consideration since his age was not below forty five 
years, is marked as Exhibit M-7. 


In cross-examination, Management Witness deposed that Notice issued to the workman for enquiry was 
served upon him but he could not produce any document in support of such service. Management Witness denied that 
the workman was less than forty five years at the time of his dismissal or that the punishment imposed was 
disproportionate to the nature of charge established against him. 


6. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative arguing the case on behalf of the dismissed workman submitted that 
the workman was suffering from illness due to which he was unable to attend duty. After recovery from illness when 
he reported for duty, he was not permitted to join and without issuing charge sheet and holding enquiry, workman has 
been held guilty of the charge of unauthorized absence and was dismissed from service. Mr. Kumar argued that due to 
absence from duty for three months and few days, the Disciplinary Authority has imposed a very harsh punishment, 
disproportionate to the alleged misconduct. He further argued that no second show cause Notice was issued to the 
workman and he was not given opportunity to get himself explained about the finding of the Enquiry Officer. 
Therefore, it is urged that order of dismissal issued against the workman is not sustainable and the order which is 
arbitrary and bad in law is liable to be set aside. It is asserted that the management should have considered the mercy 
petition submitted by the workman for his reinstatement in service and also pay the back wages to him for the period 
he was forced to remain idle. 


7. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management of ECL refuting the argument advanced by the union 
representative submitted that in the instant case, workman who was appointed in service on 26/10/1981 as Trainee at 
Ghusick Colliery remained absent from service from 11/07/1983 without any intimation or authorization for the leave. 
Workman was issued charge sheet on 09/11/1983 levelling charge under section 29(16) of Certified Standing Order 
applicable to the company for his unauthorized absence causing dislocation of company’s work and inconvenience to 
fellow workmen. It is argued that charge sheet was served upon the workman and he submitted explanation on 
12/12/1983 denying that he was absent from 11/07/1983 and admitted that he was rather absent from 03/09/1983 due 
to jaundice and he was under medical treatment from 04/10/1983 to 11/12/1983. Ld. Advocate argued that 
unauthorized absence of the workman from service during trainee period speaks volumes against his misconduct and 
the management decided to hold a domestic enquiry against him for his unauthorized absence. A copy of charge sheet 
produced by the management is marked as Exhibit M-! and reply to the charge sheet by Chhatrapati Majhi is marked 
as Exhibit M-1/1. Referring to Exhibit M-2, a letter dated 29.02.1984/02.03.1984 issued by the Manager, Ghusick 
Colliery, submitted that Mr. Samir Kr. Banerjee was the Enquiry Officer to hold enquiry in respect of charge against 
Chhatrapati Majhi. Notice of enquiry (Exhibit M-3) was issued and sent to the workman at his address under 
registered post. Copy of postal envelope is produced as Exhibit M-4. Ld. Advocate for the management further argued 
that Notice of Enquiry was transmitted to the workman under registered post and a copy of Notice was displayed on 
the Notice Board of the office fixing 29/03/1984 as the date of enquiry. Since the workman did not participate, 
Enquiry Officer held ex-parte enquiry and examined management witnesses namely Mr. S. N. Laha, Timekeeper, 
New Ghusick Unit, Mr. Zanruj Khan, W.O.’s Clerk, New Ghusick Unit and Mr. B. K. Mojumder, Compounder, New 
Ghusick Medical Unit. Though charged workman in his reply stated that he was absent from 03/09/1983 and not from 
11/07/1983, all management witnesses consistently stated before the Enquiry Officer that the workman (Chhatrapati 
Majhi) was absent from duty from 11/07/1983 till date and he did not send information to the management that he 
was suffering from illness since 11/07/1983. Ld. Advocate for the management took me through the finding of the 
Enquiry Officer where the charged workman was found guilty of charge for unauthorized absence. Admittedly, no 
second show cause Notice was issued to the workman. At the relevant time there was no binding decision upon the 
management as was later laid down by Hon’ble Supreme Court in a case of Md. Ramzan Khan Vs. Government of 
India in 1993 that Second show cause Notice was mandatory. Ld. Advocate argued that ample opportunity was 
granted to the charged employee to defend his case and the principle of Natural Justice was duly followed by the 
Enquiry Officer. Charged employee being fully aware of issuance of charge sheet on 12/12/1983 did not bother to 
find out the consequence and preferred not to participate in the enquiry only to raise dispute at a later stage. It is 
argued that the Disciplinary Authority after considering all relevant materials, charge sheet, enquiry proceeding, 


694 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [Part II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


report and evidence recorded by the Enquiry Officer, passed an order dismissing the workman from service with 
immediate effect i.e. 08/06/1984. Order of dismissal has been produced as Exhibit M-6. Ld. Advocate argued that 
punishment of dismissal is proportionate to the misconduct and the order of dismissal calls for no interference. 


8. I have considered the rival arguments advanced on behalf of the union and Id. Advocate for the management 
in the light of materials on record including pleading of parties, evidence and documents produced by the 
management as Exhibits. From evidence on record, it is well established that the aggrieved workman was appointed 
in service of the company on 26/10/1981 and it is admitted that without prior intimation or authorization of the 
management, he started absenting from duty. In the charge sheet, it is alleged that the workman absented from duty 
from 11/07/1983 whereas workman in his reply has stated that he was not absent from 11/07/1983 but failed to attend 
duty from 03/09/1983 duty due to jaundice and was under medical treatment from 04/10/1983 to 11/12/1983. 
Workman received charge sheet by putting signature on 12/12/1983 (Exhibit M-1) and he was fully aware of the 
charge of unauthorized absence levelled against him. Management of ECL not being satisfied with the explanation 
submitted by the workman against the charge decided to hold enquiry and appointed Enquiry Officer by letter dated 
02/03/1984 (Exhibit M-2). Enquiry Officer issued Notice of Enquiry on 04/03/1984 addressed to Chhatrapati Majhi 
fixing 29/03/1984 as date for enquiry and he was asked to appear at the office of the Welfare Officer at 10.30 a.m. 
along with a co-workman for assisting him. Notice of Enquiry was sent to Chhatrapati Majhi under registered post 
which was returned unserved with an endorsement “not known”. Enquiry Officer held ex-parte enquiry against 
Chhatrapati Majhi. Enquiry proceeding discloses that Notice under registered post was sent to the workman and the 
Notice was also displayed on the Notice Board before initiating enquiry proceeding. Three Management 
Representatives were examined who consistently stated that the workman was absent from 11/07/1983 till the date of 
enquiry on 29/03/1984, which continued thereafter. On a perusal of the enquiry proceeding (Exhibit M-5) and finding 
of the Enquiry Officer dated 31/03/1984 (Exhibit M-5/1) it appears to me that though enquiry was held only on one 
date, enquiry proceeding was held after giving sufficient opportunity to the charged workman who neither appeared 
nor sought for accommodation or adjournment of the proceeding to some other date. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union 
representative submitted that the workman was unable to attend duty due to his illness but no document has been 
produced by the union in support of the cause of absence of the workman. It also appears from the enquiry proceeding 
during reference that the charged employee was not examined by the union to face cross-examination by the 
management. Therefore, contention of the workman that no opportunity was given to him before his dismissal is 
unacceptable. It is to be borne in mind that if a workman who joined service in October, 1981 resorts to unauthorized 
absence within a period of two years from the date of his employment in public sector would be a serious misconduct. 


9. Even if for argument sake it is accepted that the workman was actually suffering from illness which 
prevented him from duty, workman is still duty bound to inform the management of the company about the reasons 
for which he was unable to attend duty. By not informing the management about the reasons for his absence gives rise 
to a presumption that the workman has abandoned his work under the company and he is not entitled to any benefit. 
Hon’ble High Court of Punjab and Haryana in the case of Nav Bharat Industries Vs. Presiding Officer and 
another [2018 LLR 439], held that if a workman remained absent unauthorizedly for a long time, even due to his 
illness without informing the management about it, termination of his service by invoking conditions as stipulated in 
the Certified Standing Orders, is not illegal. It was further held that when the workman does not inform the 
management about his illness, remaining absent for a long time, presumption drawn by the management that the 
workman has abandoned his job of his own is justified. From the settled position of law, it would emerge that 
unauthorized absence compounded with non-communication of the reason of absence to the management are 
unacceptable and workman cannot derive any benefit of doubt. An illness cannot be ground for unauthorized absence 
and the workman would not be entitled to benefits. Workman in the instant case was dismissed from service in June, 
1984 and he raised Industrial Dispute before Government of India, Ministry of Labour only in the year 2013 i.e. after 
a lapse of twenty nine years. Prior to that, workman had filed application before the management for reinstatement on 
04/11/2009 and the application was not found fit for consideration as Chhatrapati Majhi was not less than forty five 
years of age which was a basic requirement for re-employment in terms of Memorandum of Settlement dated 
22/05/2007. In a case of Prabhakar Vs. Joint Director, Sericulture Department and Another [(2015) 15 SCC 1] 
it was laid down by the Hon’ble Court in Paragraph fourteen that “to summarize, although there is no limitation 
prescribed under the Act for making reference under Section 10 (1) of the Industrial Disputes Act, yet it is for the 
“appropriate government” to consider whether it is expedient or not to make the reference. The words “at any time” 
used in section 10(1) do not admit of any limitation in making an order of reference and laws of limitation are not 
applicable to proceedings under the Industrial Disputes Act. However, the policy of industrial adjudication is that 
very stale claim should not be generally encouraged or allowed in as much as unless there is satisfactory explanation 
for delay as, apart from obvious risk of industrial peace from the entertainment of claims after long lapse of time, it is 
necessary also to take into account the unsettling effect which it is likely to have on the employers financial 
arrangement and to avoid dislocation of an industry.” From the aforesaid decision it is pertinent to decide whether 
the Industrial Dispute raised by the union/workman is within a reasonable time from the cause of action or the same 
has been initiated after a very long period without explanation of delay. In the instant case, workman is absolutely 
oblivious about his disengagement since issuance of order of dismissal in June, 1984. After passage of more than 
twenty five years, workman sought for reinstatement in service and raised an Industrial Dispute after a period of 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 695 


twenty nine years. There is no explanation as to why such inordinate delay occurred. Considering facts and 
circumstances of the case I hold that the Industrial Dispute raised by the workman has no merit in seeking relief. 
There is no cogent ground to explain long delay caused by the union in raising dispute. Therefore, I find no reason to 
interfere with the order of dismissal of the workman. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 
that the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest against Chhatrapati Majhi who is represented by Koyala Mazdoor 
Congress. Dismissed workman is not entitled to any relief in this case. Let an Award be drawn up in the light of my 


above discussion. Let copies of Award be communicated to the Ministry for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 27 जनवरी, 2026 


का.आ. 116.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार एस.ई.सी.एल.के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण-सह-श्रम न्यायालय, जबलपुर के पंचाट(एलसी/आर-70/2019) को yeh 
करती है, जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को 27/01/2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था। 


[सं. एल-22013/01/2026-आई.आर. (सीएम-1)] 


सलोनी, उप निदेशक/लिंक अधिकारी 
New Delhi, the 27th January, 2026

Management 


(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 57 day of December, 2025) 


As per letter dated 25/10/2019 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’) as per 
Notification No. J-1(3-2)/2019-IR dt. 25/10/2019. The dispute under reference relates to:- 


“एस्रईसीएलत, प्रबंधन CANT ठडेलीगेशन ऑफ पावर (Delegation of Power) # व्वारा स्थाई आदेशों में किए गए 
संशोधन संविधान के अनुच्छेव 317 (1) के अंतर्गत विधिक उप से उचित है या नहीं? यवि नहीं, तो एसर्डस़ीएल् प्रबंधन 
के क्षेत्रीय महाप्रबंधकों उपक्षेत्रीय प्रबंधकों तथा खबान प्रबंधकों के CANT उन संशोधित स्थाई आदेशों से प्राप्त शक्तियों 
का प्रयोग करते हुए की गई अनुशासनात्मक कार्यवाहियाँ विधिक उप से उचित & या नहीं एवं TASH प्रबंधन व्वारा 
उन संशोधनों पर तत्कान wera से प्रतिबंध न त्रगाना उचित है अथवा नहीं? ale नहीं तो संबंधित सभी कर्मकार 
जिनको संशोधित स्थाई आवेशों से प्राप्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए सेवा से TACT किया गया हैं; वह FAT अनुतोष 
पाने के हकवार है?” 


After registering the case on the basis of reference, notices were issued to the parties. They appeared and 
filed their respective statement of claim in defense. 


It is the case of the workers Union that it is registered union under the Trade Union Act, having registration 
No. NGP/4672, issued by Competent Authority and is authorized to raise disputes on behalf of its members. The 
applicant is Secretary of the said Union, authorized by the Union on this behalf. According to the Union, the 
management of S.E.C.L. has notified its Certified Standing Orders, made under the provisions of the Jndustrial 
Employment (Standing Orders) Act, 1946, which is applicable to the management and employees of the company, no 
amendment can be made in these Certified Standing Orders without permission of Competent Authority and if there is 
any proposal for amendment, it requires to be approved by the Central Government, who is the Competent Authority 
but the management of S.E.C.L. has made amendments neither without such approval nor by getting approved by the 
Competent Authority which is illegal and arbitrary. Further, rules specifically provide that the appropriate 
government, by notification in official Gazette, may direct that any power exercised by it under rules there under shall 
in relation to such matter and subject to such conditions, if any, that may be specified in this direction to be exercised 
by such Officer or Authority subordinate. The management has made amendments permitting delegation of power by 
way of authorizing Subordinate Officer to take disciplinary action against the employees without the approval of 
Appropriate Government/ Central Government. Hence, such amendments are bad in law. 


Furthermore, under clause 2.3 of the Certified Standing Orders, it has been provided that ‘Competent 
Authority’ means an Officer specifically nominated by Chairman/Managing Director concerned by an order in writing 
for the purpose of standing orders, such orders shall be put on notice-board and copies sent to concerned registered 
Tribunal whereas in the case in hand, no such amendment was put on notice-board nor was it communicated to any 
Union. Thus, disciplinary action against employees, taken by Subordinate Officers has no sanction of law. 

It is also the case of Union that the Director Personnel, is Appointing Authority of the employees whereas 
the standing orders have been amended providing delegation of powers to terminate or punish employees to Officers 
Subordinate to the Appointing Authority which is in violation of Article 311(1) of the Constitution of India, hence, 
bad in law. 

The delegation of power and amendments in the model standing orders made by the management authorizing 
the Unit Head and Section Head to take disciplinary action against the employees is in violation of Article 311(1) 
because they are Officers Subordinate to the Appointing Authority which is Director Personnel of these amendments 
made on various dates i.e., 09.12.1991, 12.12.1991, 16.12.1991, 31.03.2008 and 01.04.2008, nominating various 
authorities for taking disciplinary action and punishment lower than the Appointing Authority is against constitution, 
hence, unjust, illegal and arbitrary. The workman Union has thus prayed that holding these delegations as mentioned 
vide amendment orders mentioned above de-horse of Article 301 of the Constitution of India, disciplinary actions 
including the terminations, dismissals and removals passed by different authorities of the management company be 
held illegal and they be held entitled to be reinstated with all back wages, in service as well post retiral benefits.
