AWARD 


The Government of India, Ministry of Labour Vide order No.L-12012/56/2014-IR (B-I) dated 17" 
November 2014, as referred this industrial dispute under class D of sub Section(1) and Sub section (2) A of section 
10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, for adjudication by this tribunal. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 713 


1.“Whether the action of management of Andhra Pradesh Grameen Vikas Bank, represented by Regional Manager 
Srikakulam, in terminating the services of Sri Baswa Govinda Raju, ex-casual messenger, Vendangi branch, is legal 
and justified? If not, what relief the workman concerned entitled to?” 


2. After receipt of the aforementioned reference, it was numbered as ID No. 246 of 2014, and notices were issued to 
both the workman and the management and same was served on both the parties. 


In response to the notice, Petitioner appeared and filed claim statement with the averment that he was 
appointed as casual messenger on April 1997 in the Vandrangi Branch of Respondent bank on payment of Rs. 10/- 
per day. Further, it is averred that he worked continuously without any break to the entire satisfaction of his superior 
officer, and there was no complaint whatsoever nature against the petitioner in his entire service. Further, it is alleged 
that Respondent did not issue any appointment letter or pay slip to the petitioner during his service, and petitioner 
used to sign on the voucher at the time of taking salary. The salary was sometimes credited to the petitioner's savings 
bank account in the Vandrangi branch. Further, it is submitted that the Respondent management deducted the amount 
from the petitioner's salary towards the contribution of the provident fund for the months of June, July, and August 
2012. Further, Petitioner alleged that the Respondent has terminated the petitioner from his service by order dated 107 
July 2013 without conducting any inquiry, notice pay, or compensation or conducting any inquiry. The termination 
order of the petitioner is illegal, arbitrary, and contrary to the provision of the Industrial Disputes Act. Further, it is 
alleged that Respondent has terminated the petitioner from service in violation of the provision of sections 25 F, 25 G, 
and 25 H of Industrial Disputes Act. Further, it is alleged that Petitioner requested the Respondent several times for 
reinstatement, but the Respondent keep postponing the matter on some pretext or the other. The petitioner gave 
representation dated 20" January 2014 to the Deputy Commissioner of Labour, Srikakulam, requesting him to 
intervene in the matter and to direct the Respondent to reinstate the petitioner in service. Further, it is alleged that the 
petitioner made a representation dated 20" January 2014 to the Assistant Labour Commissioner, Visakhapatnam, 
requesting to intervene the matter, and Assistant Labour Commissioner issued notices to the Respondent for joint 
meeting- conciliation proceeding but the Respondent refused to reinstate the petitioner into service. After several 
meetings, the Assistant Labour Commissioner, Visakhapatnam, close the conciliation meeting and sent the figure 
report to the government of India. Therefore, prayed to direct the respondent to reinstate the petitioner in his service 
with full back wages by setting aside the termination order. 


3.Per contra, Respondent has filed counter. He has contended that the petitioner was engaged as a casual 
labour by the respondent to meet the exigencies of the work, and soon after the exigency were met, the term of casual 
labour would automatically come to an end. Therefore, the same cannot be described as a termination of services by 
the Respondent. Further, it is contended that undisputedly Petitioner was engaged on casual basis, but the petitioner 
has no right to claim continuity of permanency of the service. 


Further, it is submitted that during the engagement of Petitioner as a casual labour, there were many complaints 
against the petitioner while working as at the Vandrangi branch, and he has committed the irregularities and certain 
misconduct. The petitioner also availed subsidy loans in the name of his relative in a clandestine manner. Further, it is 
alleged that while Petitioner was engaged as a casual labour on daily wages at Respondent branch, he has resorted to 
fraudulent activities such as taking loan from the customers of the bank, misguiding bank customer not to repay the 
loan, and also accepting the credit moves made by the customers towards bank deposit without the knowledge of the 
bank staff. Therefore, it is prayed to dismiss the petition as not maintainable. 


In support of the petition, petitioner has filed oral evidence, has produced witness WW1, and also filed 
documentary evidence, exhibit W1 to W10. These documents can be described copy of the termination letter dated 
10" July 2013, exhibit W1, copy of the letter issued by DLC dated 18" July 2014, exhibit W2, copy of the 
representation to ALC, Visakhapatnam, exhibit W3, copy of the wage particulars from exhibit 4 to exhibit 8, copy and 
of the PF contribution voucher exhibit W9; copy of the PF contribution voucher exhibit W10; and copy of the bank 
passbook exhibit W11. On the other hand, Respondent has examined witness MW1, and no documentary evidence 
was filed. 


4.Heard the argument of the learned counsel Petitioner for either parties, Petitioner has also filed written 
arguments. 


on the basis of Pleadings and submissions of either parties, the following issues emerge for determination 
in this case: 


1. Whether the action of the management of Andhra Pradesh Grameena Vikas Bank, represented by Regional 
Manager Srikakulam in terminating the services of Shri B. Govinda Raju ex-casual messenger, Vandrangi 
branch, is legal and justified? 


2. To what relief, if any, workman is entitled to? 
Finding 
Issue No. I:- 


Petitioner has alleged that he was appointed as a casual messenger in April 1997 on payment of Rs. 10 per 
day and had worked continuously without any break to the entire satisfaction of the superior officer. Further, it is 


714 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


alleged that Petitioner has terminated from service by the Respondent vide order dated 107 July 13, without 
conducting an inquiry, notice pay, and compensation, in violation of provisions contained under sections 25F, 25G, 
and 25H of the Industrial Disputes Act. on the other hand, Respondent has denied the allegation and contended that 
the petitioner was engaged as a casual labour by the Respondent to meet exigencies of the work, and soon after the 
exigencies are met, the term of casual labour automatically would also come to an end. Therefore, same cannot be 
described as a termination of service by the Respondent. It is undisputed that the petitioner had worked in the 
Respondent branch as a casual labour on a daily wages basis. Now, we would examine the legality of the termination 
of the petitioner from service by the Respondent. 


In this regard, the relevant provisions contained under section 25F of the ID Act are extracted as here under:- 


25-F. Conditions precedent to retrenchment of workmen:-No workman employed in any industry who has been in 
continuous service for not less than one year under an employer shall be retrenched by that employer until- 


(a) the workman has been given one month's notice in writing indicating the reasons for retrenchment and the 
period of notice has expired, or the workman has been paid in lieu of such notice, wages for the period of the 
notice. 


[xxx] 


(b) the workman has been paid, at the time of retrenchment, compensation which shall be equivalent to fifteen days' 
average pay [for every completed year of continuous service] or any part thereof in excess of six months; and 


(c) notice in the prescribed manner is served on the appropriate Government [or such authority as may be specified by 
the appropriate Government by notification in the Official Gazette]. 


AS per provision, contained the 25F, the condition precedent for retrenchment is that the petitioner had 
worked for 240 days continuously in a 12-month just preceding the date of his termination, and burden of proof lies 
upon the shoulders of the petitioner to prove this fact. In support of his allegation, the petitioner has examined himself 
as WW1, and in chief statement affidavit, the petitioner has stated that he was appointed as casual messenger on April 
1997 in the Respondent bank branch and had worked continuously without any break to the entire satisfaction of the 
superior officers, and he has been terminated from service by the Respondent by vide order dated 10" July 2013. This 
witness was cross-examined by the Respondent's counsel, and in his cross-examination, WWI has stated that he 
joined in the Respondent bank on a daily wage basis, and for the first two years, he was paid weekly. Further, witness 
State WW1 states that he was regularly employed but was not issued any appointment letter from the Respondent 
bank. WWI states that he used to allotted work on a daily basis and used to file the vouchers. Further, witness WW 1 
state that initially, he was getting wages in the form of cash, and he used to get pay slips for the wages drawn by him. 
He has filed the pay slip. The document filed by the petitioner are: Exhibit W1 is the copy of the termination order, 
which goes reveals that the petitioner, B. Govinda Raju, was engaged as a casual labour, and the competent authority 
has decided to discontinue his services as a casual labour with immediate effect vide letter dated 10" July, 2013. 
Furthermore, Ex: W5 to W9 are the copy of the wage particulars of the Petitioner workman. The perusal of Ex WS, 
Ex १४6, Ex W7, Ex W8, Ex W9 goes to reveal that the Respondent has paid wages to the Petitioner for the month of 
January 2013, February 2013, March 2013, April 2013, May 2013, according to number of days he has worked. 
Further, it goes to reveal that Petitioner has worked as a daily wager for these 5 months and he has been paid daily 
wages accordingly. 


Petitioner failed to produce any cogent and reliable evidence to prove the fact that he had worked for 240 days 
continuously with the Respondent in a calendar year just preceding from date of his termination i.e., 10" July 2013. 
Therefore, Petitioner failed to discharge his burden of proof to prove the fact of 240 days of continuous service which 
is imperative condition of retrenchment as enshrined under section 25F of the ID Act. 


Thus, petitioner has failed to discharge the burden of proof regarding 240 days of continuous service just 
preceding the date of his termination. Whereas Respondent has examined witness MW1 in support of his contention, 
who has categorically in his chief statement has stated categorically that petitioner was engaged as a casual labour by 
the Respondents, and the petitioner was well aware of the fact that the engagement is not permanent. Therefore, there 
is no permanency of the service, and hence the question of termination of the petitioner's service by the Respondent 
does not arise. Further, witness MW1 states that as soon as the exigency of the work is completed, the person engaged 
for the purpose of such work would naturally become redundant. Hence, the engagement of such a person is for a 
specific purpose, and the person so engaged cannot claim either continuity or permanency or treatment on par of with 
the regular employees. Further, this witness, MW1, was cross-examined by the petitioner counsel, but nothing has 
been elicited in cross-examination to discredit the testimony of the witness MW1. 


Further, the Respondent has also relied upon the decision of the Supreme Court in the case of Himanshu 
Kumar Vidyarthi Vs State of Bihar, date of decision 26" March 1997, wherein the Honourable Supreme Court held 
that daily wagers have no right to post, and their disengagement is not arbitrary. Since they are temporary employees 
working on daily wages, their engagement from service cannot be treated under Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 


Thus, in view of the above, the petitioner has failed to discharge his burden of proof that he has been 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 715 


retrenched in contravention of the provisions contained in Section 250) of the ID Act. As regards the burden of proof, 
certain decisions of the Honourable Supreme Court are Relevant regarding Burden of Proof In case of Retrenchment, 
of workman which are being discussed as hereunder: - 


(a). In Rajasthan State Ganganagar S. Mills Ltd. v. State of Rajasthan and Anr. (2004) Apex Court held: 


"It was the case of the workman that he had worked for more than 240 days in the year concerned. This 
claim was denied by the appellant. It was for the claimant to lead evidence to show that he had worked for 240 days in 
the year preceding the date of his termination. He has filed an affidavit. It is statement which is in his favor and that 
cannot be regarded as sufficient evidence for any Court or Tribunal to come to the conclusion that in fact the claimant 
had worked for 240 days in a year." 


(b).In the case of Mohan Lal v. Management, BEL 1981 SCC P. 225. Hon'ble Apex Court held:- 


“Before a workman can claim retrenchment, not being in consonance of Section 25 of the ID act he has to 
show that he has been in continuous service of not less than 1 year with the employer who had retrenched him from 
service The Hon'ble Apex Court in this case also laid down the principle of how to count 240 days of service within 
one year and held: "Clause (2)(a) provides for a fiction to treat a workman in continuous service for a period of one 
year despite the fact that he has not rendered uninterrupted service for a period of one year but he has rendered service 
period of 240 days during the period of 12 calendar months for counting backwards and just preceding the relevant 
date being the date of retrenchment. In other words, in order to invoke the fiction enacted in clause (2)(a) it is 
necessary to determine first the relevant date, ie the date of termination of service which is complained of as 
retrenchment. After that date is ascertained move backward to a period of 12 months just preceding the date of 
retrenchment and then ascertain whether within the period of 12 months, the workman has rendered service for a 
period of 240 days. If these three facts are affirmatively answered in favor of the workman pursuant to the deeming 
fiction enacted in clause (2)(a) it will have to be assumed that the workman is in continuous service for a period of 
one year and he will satisfy the eligibility qualification enacted in Section 25-F”’ 


(c).In the case of GM., BSNL and others V. Mahesh Chand AIR 2008 SC (Supp) 1328, wherein the Hon'ble Apex 
Court have held, 


"It was the case of the claimant that he had so worked but this claim was denied by the appellant. It was then 
for the claimant to lead evidence to show that he had in fact worked for 240 days in the year preceding his 
termination. Filing of an affidavit is only his own statement in his favour and that cannot be regarded as 
sufficient evidence forany Court or Tribunal to come to the conclusion that a workman had, in fact, worked for 240 
days in a year. No proof of receipt of salary or wages for 240 days or order or record of appointment or engagement 
for this period was produced by the workman. On this ground alone, the award is liable to be set aside." 


(d). In the case of Range Forest Officer Vs. S T Hadimani AIR 2002 SC page 1147, wherein Hon'ble Apex Court 
have held, 


"the onus lies upon claimant to show that he had in fact worked for 240 days in a year-In absence of proof of 
receipt of salary or wages record of appointment, filing of an affidavit by workman is not sufficient evidence to prove 
that he had worked for 240 days in a year preceding his termination.” 


(e). In the case of Essen Deinki Vs. Rajiv Kumar, AIR 2003 SC 38 the Hon'ble Apex Court have held, 


"It was for the employee concerned to prove that he has in fact completed 240 days in the last preceding 12 
months’ period." 


(f). In the case of Municipal Corporation, Faridabad Vs. Siri Niwas AIR 2004 SC 4681, wherein Hon'ble Apex Court 
have held:- 


"that the burden of proof was on the workman to show that he had worked for 240 days in the preceding 12 
months prior to his alleged retrenchment u/s 25F of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. It is also held that it is 
improbable that a person working in a Local Authority would not be in possession of any documentary evidence to 
support his claim before the tr. Apart from muster rolls he could have shown the terms and conditions of his offer of 
appointment and the remuneration received by him for working during the afore mentioned period. He even did not 
examine any other witness in support of his case." 


(g). In the case of Chief Engineer, Ranjit Sagar Dam and another Vs. Sham Lal, 2006 AIR SCW 3574, wherein the 
Hon'ble Apex Court have held: 


"Fact that workman had worked for 240 days or more in year immediately preceding termination Burden of proof 
Burden is on workman not on employer-delay of nine years in seeking-no relief could be given to claimant." 
Manager, RBI, Bangalore vs. S. Mani & Ors., on 14.3.2005 AIR 2005 Supreme Court 2179 have held that, merely the 
completion of 240 days of continuous service by the workmen by itself does not give rise to claim of permanence in 
the employment Hon'ble Supreme Court have held that:- 


716 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


"It may not be out of place to mention that completion of 240 days of continuous service in a year may not 
by itself be a ground for directing an order of regularization. It is also not the case of the Respondents that they were 
appointed in accordance with the extant rules. No direction for regularization of their services was, therefore, 
could be issued." 


Therefore, in view of the foregoing discussion and law laid down by the Honourable court as discussed 
above, I am of the considered opinion that the Petitioner has failed to establish that his services have been terminated 
by the Respondent in contravention of provision of section 25F, 25G,25H of the Act. Thus, this issue is decided 
against the petitioner. 


Issue No. II:- 


In view of the foregone discussion and finding given at Issue No. I, Petitioner failed to establish that 
Respondent management has terminated him services illegally. Therefore, the action of the Respondent management 
in terminating the services of the petitioner held legal and justified. Therefore, petitioner has not entitled to any relief, 
and petition deserved to be dismissed. 


AWARD 


In view of findings given at Issue No. I and I, the action of Respondent management in terminating the 
services of petitioner, B. Govinda Raju vide order dated 10-07-2013 is held legal and justified. The Petition is devoid 
of merit hence stands dismissed. Reference is answered accordingly. 


Transmit as per provision of I.D Act.

November 2025. 


IRFAN QAMAR, Presiding Officer

ORDER 


Present petition has been filed by the Petitioner, C. Tilaknandan, against the Respondent Chairman and 
Managing Director, State Bank of Hyderabad, Gun Foundry Hyderabad, and Chief Manager Personnel and Assistant 
General Manager of the State Bank of Hyderabad, under Section 2 A (2) of the Industrial Disputes Act 1947, with the 
prayer to declare the action of the Respondent vide proceeding dated 220 March 2011 informing the Petitioner that 
competent authority advised that he has voluntarily vacated of service with effect from 21“ April 2011, is bad in law 
arbitrary and violation of the principles of natural justice hence, liable to be said aside. 


2. Notice served upon the Respondent. The Respondent filed counter. 


3. The fact in belief culled out from the records are that the Petitioner after his retirement from the Indian 
Army, applied for recruitment to the post of watchman in the State Bank of Hyderabad, and in pursuance of the 
process of selection he was selected to the post of watchman cadre under subordinate cadre vide order dated 
21st February 1998. After completion of probation period of six months successfully he was confirmed on the post 


718 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SECc. 3(ii)] 


vide order dated 80 October 1998. Further, Petitioner appeared for a promotional examination from subordinate to 
clerical cadre and he appeared in interview for the post of clerical cadre on 10" July 2003, and got selected as clerk 
in the year 2003, and was transferred and posted to Huzurabad Branch, Karimnagar. 


Further, it is submitted that Petitioner was regularly elected as a Bench Secretary in successive elections of 
the State Bank of Hyderabad Staff Association in the year 2007, and due to said reason Petitioner was subjected to 
harassment and a vindictive transfer for his activity in the association. He was subjected transferred from the 
Huzurabad branch to Moula Ali branch, Hyderabad, vide order dated 5' December 2009. Petitioner joined the 
transferred place but, after working until 27th May 2010, he was forced to be absent due to ill health. While so, 
Petitioner was issued a memo dated 28" June 2010 stating that was absent from duty from 27" May 2010 without 
informing the bank. It is submitted that Petitioner was alone at home and as such he could not inform the Respondent 
about his ill health. As the Petitioner was undergoing treatment in a private hospital, he could not receive the notice 
from the Respondent. As the Petitioner received the notice dated 28" June 2010, he submitted a representation dated 
18th February 2011, informing the Respondent that he was not well and intended to join immediately after his 
recovery from ill health. Petitioner submitted that the Respondent bank authority, SBH Region, Third Zonal Office, 
Secunderabad, vide its letter dated 227 March 2011, informed the Petitioner that the competent authority has 
unilaterally taken a decision that the Petitioner has voluntarily vacated the service with effect from 24" January 2011 
and advising the Petitioner not to have any further correspondence in this regard. Petitioner further submit that he was 
undergoing the cardiological problem from the mid-2010, which has resulted in breathlessness, chest pain, and other 
related sudden developments in his health. Petitioner has joined the Sigma Hospital Secunderabad, on 9" February 
2011, and after undergoing treatment there up to March 2011, was advised complete bed rest. But again he has 
developed the same problem in the month of February 2012 and again he was admitted for treatment in Yashoda 
Hospital, Secunderabad, on 24" February 2012, and got discharged on 28" February 2012, Petitioner has enclosed all 
the health certificates in evidence as Annexure X. Petitioner was continuously undergoing treatment for cardiac 
problem from January 2011 to till June 2012, and Respondent has decleared Petitioner as he has_ voluntarily vacated 
the service with effect from 24" July 2011 holding that he was unauthorizedly absent from 25" July 2010, vide letter 
dated 25" January 2012, Petitioner has rendered spotless service from 30th March 1998 till 27" May 2010 and he has 
been in the employment of the Respondent in a confirmed post. Accepting the fact that he was absent from duty on 
medical ground Petitioner has not committed any illegality. Respondent having full knowledge of the precarious 
health condition of the Petitioner treated him as unauthorizedly absent and also unilaterally declared that he 
voluntarily vacated the service without any such voluntary action on the part of the Petitioner. Respondent did not 
issue any memo to Petitioner and not called for any explanation and did not prefer to hold any inquiry into the subject. 
The action of Respondent vide order dated 22" March 2011, intimating the Petitioner that the competent authority has 
unilaterally decided that the Petitioner has voluntarily vacated the service with effect from 24th January 2011, is 
absolutely illegal, arbitrary, and violative of the principles of natural justice. Petitioner prayed that the action of 
Respondent vide letter dated 227 March 2011, informing the Petitioner that competent authority advised the 
Petitioner that he has voluntarily vacated the services with effect from 217 April 2011 is illegal and liable to be set 
aside and Respondent be directed to reinstate the Petitioner into service with all consequential benefits, such as pay 
and allowances, continuity of service, and seniority. 


4. Per contra Respondent has filed counter and has contended therein that present industrial dispute raised by 
the Petitioner is not maintainable either in law or in fact. Respondent denies all the allegations made in the claim 
petition except those that are specifically admitted therein. Further, it is contended that Petitioner was selected for the 
post of watchman, and he joined the service on 237 March 1996. Further, Petitioner appeared for the promotional 
examination from subordinate to clerical cadre and was promoted to the post of clerical cadre in the year 2003, he was 
transferred and posted at Huzurabad branch, Karimnagar, as award staff in 2003. Petitioner was transferred to Moulali 
branch, Hyderabad, in the year 2009, after working for around six years in the Huzurabad branch. Petitioner was not 
subjected to any harassment or vindictive transfer for his union activities in the association. 


Further, Respondent contended that Petitioner himself admit that he was absent from duties after 27" May 
2010, and he has not made any communication with his superior-Respondent bank and did not report for duty in spite 
of receiving the notice dated 287 June 2010. The allegation of the Petitioner that he was absent due to ill health is 
self-contradictory, as on the one hand, the Petitioner states that he was alone at home, and on the other hand, the 
Petitioner states that he was undergoing treatment in the private hospital and as such he could not receive the notice. It 
is pertinent to note that the Petitioner is silent about the nature of the illness, treatment and details of the alleged 
private hospital, etc., which itself establishes that the Petitioner is not coming out with the true and correct facts. 
Further, it is contended that the Petitioner was absent from duties from 27" May 2010, without any intimation and 
first notice was served on the Petitioner on 247 November 2010, directing him to report for duty within 30 days from 
the date of the notice and same was acknowledged by him. In spite of receipt of first notice, Petitioner failed to report 
for duty within the stipulated period. Thereafter, second notice was served on the Petitioner on 247 December 2010, 
calling upon the Petitioner to report for duty within 30 days from the date of receipt of the said notice. Even after 
receipt of second notice Petitioner has neither reported to duty nor sent any intimation to the bank about his absence 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 719 


from duty. Further it is contended that Petitioner submitted a representation dated 187 February 2011, only after 
receiving the advice from the competent authority that the Petitioner has voluntarily vacated his service vide letter 
dated 247 January 2011. Further, it is submitted that as per (33) of the VIII Bipartite settlement dated 2" June 2005, 
an employee is deemed to have voluntarily vacated his employment when he exceeds 90 days of absence in spite of 
notice. Thus, the dismissal of the employee was in accordance with Bipartite settkement and in terms of extend 
guidelines, vide H.O. circular No. PR 2006/2007/60 dated 317 October 2006. 


Further, it is contended that alleged medical record submitted by Petitioner pertain to the period after his 
voluntary vacation of service and not relevant. Respondent bank has followed the procedure strictly in accordance 
with the law and also guidelines in issuance of the termination proceeding against the Petitioner. Therefore, 
Respondent bank has not committed any illegality or irregularity in terminating the service of the Petitioner. 
Respondent has prayed to dismiss the claim of the Petitioner and to pass nil award. 


5: In support of the averment of claim petition, Petitioner has filed chief statement, affidavit of witness WW1, 
and has also filed documents in support of claim, which has been exhibited as Exhibit W1 to W12. On the contrary 
Respondent has filed chief statement affidavit of the witness MW1, and also filed the document Exhibit M1, M2, M3, 
M4, M5, M6. Both sides have also filed their written submissions. 


6. Ongoing pleading and submission of either party, following issues emerge for determination in 
present case: - 


I. Whether the action of Respondent management vide letter number R3/GR1/Staff/3576 dated 24.01.2011, 
informing the Petitioner that since Petitioner have failed to report for duty, therefore, he is deemed to have 
voluntarily vacated his employment from 24.01.2011, as per clause 33 of the Eight Bipartite settlement 
dated the 274 June 2005, circulated vide Head Officer Circular no. PER/2005-06/16 dated the 17 June 2005, 
is legal and justified? 


II. To what relief, if any Petitioner is entitled to? 
Findings 
ISSUE NO. I: - 


hs Petitioner in his Petition has claimed that he was posted as a clerk in the year 2003 and transferred to the 
Huzurabad branch, Karimnagar and thereafter, vide letter dated 570 December 2009, he was transferred from 
Huzurabad branch to the Moula Ali branch, Hyderabad, and he joined duty at transferred place. But after working 
there up to 277 May 2010, he was absent from duty due to ill health, and has undergone the treatment of cardiac 
problem. Respondent has treated him as unauthorizedly absent from duty and vide letter dated 227 March 2011, has 
informed him that the Petitioner had voluntarily vacated the services with effect from 24" January 2011. The reason 
for his absence from duty was his illness for which he was undergoing treatment. 


8. In order to corroborate his avernment Petitioner has filed the Chief examination statement of WW1 and 
therein WW1 has reiterated the averment made in claim petition. Further, WWI was cross-examined by the 
Respondent's counsel and in cross-examination, WWI has stated that It is true that he was promoted from sub- 
ordinate cadre to clerical cadre in year 2003 and was posted at Huzurabad branch, Karimnagar District. Thereafter, he 
was transferred to Moulali branch in the year 2009. WW1 states that it is true that he worked from 2003 to 2009 at 
Huzurabad Branch. It is true that he was elected as Branch Secretary in 2007, and he cannot say about contents of 
para-No.8 of his affidavit regarding unfair labour practice and frequent transfers. Further, WW1 state it is true that 
through letter dated 28-6-2010 he was called to join the duty immediately which is marked as Ex. M1. It is true that 
vide Notice dated 24.11.2010 he was advised to report to duty within 30 days. The copy of the same is marked as 
Ex. M2. It is true that he was issued with 2nd notice dated 24.12.2010 advising him to report to duty within 30 days 
from the date of notice which is marked as Ex. M3. Further, WW1 states that It is true that he was issued with a letter 
dated 24.1.2011 stating that Petitioner vacated employment voluntarily as per clause 33 of VIII Bipartite settlement 
dated 2.6.2005, and that is marked as Ex. M4. It is true that after receipt of Ex. M4, he made a representation dated 
18.2.2011 to Respondent and Respondent gave a reply dated 22.3.2011 which is marked as Ex. MS. WW1 states that 
he is not aware about the circular dated 31.10.2006 issued by the Respondent. It is not true to suggest that he had not 
made any representation during 27.5.2010 to 24.1.2011 regarding his illness. Witness adds that he was admitted in 
Hospital due to Heart Stroke and his wife and son had informed the Respondent and in turn Respondent had issued a 
letter in favor of M/s Sigma Hospital for free treatment. WW 1 admitted that he not filed copy of such letter before 
this court It is not true to suggested that Petitioner's wife and son approached or intimated the bank regarding illness 
of Petitioner, and he is deposing false. Further, WW1 admitted that it is true that I have not made any representation 
to the Respondent regarding my illness during 27.5.2010 to 24.1.2011 even after receipt of notices from Respondent 
regarding my unauthorized absence. 


9. Thus, from the above statement of WW1 in cross-examination it is established that the Petitioner did not 
make any representation during the period of his absence from duty commencing from 27" May 2010 to 247 January 
2011, further he did not intimate to Respondent regarding his illness or apply for permission or leave. Further, WW 1 


720 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


also admitted the to recover notice dated 28" June 2010, called him to join the duty immediately and also notice dated 
247 November 2010 whereby he was again advised to report for duty within 30 days. Further, witness WW1 has 
admitted that even after receipt of notice from Respondent for his unauthorized absence from duty, he did not make 
any representation to the Respondent regarding his illness. Petitioner has also filed document in evidence, but these 
documents do not pertain to the period of untheorized absence of Petitioner from his duty. Petitioner has not filed 
any document in evidence to justify his absence from duty during the 1“ and 2" notice sent by Respondent and 
acknowledged by Petitioner i.e., Moreover, he could not produce any document. Prescription and Medical Certificate 
from treating doctor in evidence pertaining to his illness and treatment during his absenteeism period from duty 
between 27.05.2010 to 24.01.2011. Thus, for the want of relevant and cogent evidence, the plea of Petitioner that due 
to illness he was absent from duty is not tenable. 


10. On the contrary, Respondent has filed chief statement affidavit of witness MW1 and in his sworn testimony 
MW'1 has stated that Petitioner was transferred to Moula Ali branch in the year 2009 and his joined his duty and 
worked up to 27" May 2010. Further, MW1 stated that the Petitioner himself admits that he was absent from duties 
after 27 May 2010, and he has not made any communication with his superiors of Respondent bank and did not report 
for duty in spite of receiving notice dated 28" June 2010. Further, witness stated that the allegation of the Petitioner 
that he was absent due to ill health is self-contradictory, as on the one hand, the Petitioner states that he was alone at 
home, and on the other hand, he stated that he was undergoing treatment in a private hospital and as such he could not 
receive the notice. Further, Petitioner is silent about the nature of the illness and about the treatment and details of the 
alleged private hospital, etc. In the absence of such evidence the plea of the Petitioner is not established. Further, the 
witness MW1 states that the Petitioner was absent from duty since 27.05. 2010 without any information. Respondent 
served the first notice to the Petitioner on 247 November 2010, directing him to report for duty within 30 days from 
the date of the notice, and the same was acknowledged by him. In spite of receipt of the notice, the Petitioner failed to 
report for the duty within said period. Therefore, a second notice was served on the Petitioner on 247 December 2010, 
calling upon the Petitioner to report for duty within 30 days from the date of receipt of the said notice. But even after 
receipt of the said second notice, Petitioner neither reported to duty nor sent any intimation to the bank about his 
absence from duty. Further, witness MW1 stated that, as per clause 33 of the VIII bipartite settlement dated 2™ June 
2005, an employee is deemed to have voluntarily vacated his employment when he exceeds 90 days of absence in 
spite of the notice. Hence, the dismissal of the employee via letter dated 24" January 2011 was in accordance with the 
bipartite settlement and in terms of extant guidelines vide H.O. circular dated 31-10-2006. Further, the witness stated 
that the allegation of the illness period, treatment, and hospitalization, etc., of the Petitioner is self-contradictory and 
cannot be and is not believable. Further, MW1 states that the Petitioner himself has specifically admitted in his 
petition that he was under treatment from January 2011 till June 2012, whereas Petitioner was absent from duty since 
27" May 2010 without any information/sanction of leave. MW1 states that alleged medical records submitted by 
Petitioner pertains to the date after his voluntary vacation of the service, and no other particulars have been submitted 
by the Petitioner. MW1 states that Petitioner was given sufficient time to explain the reason for his unauthorized 
absence. Respondent bank has followed the procedure strictly in accordance with the law and also the guidelines in 
vogue before issuance of the termination proceeding against the Petitioner. Thus, the Respondent bank has not 
committed any illegality. In terminating the services of petitioner. 


11. Witness MW1 has also exhibited the documents in evidence Exhibit M1; Exhibit M2, M3, M4, M5; and 
M6. The perusal of the document Ex. M1 goes to show that notice dated 287 June 2010 was sent by the Respondent to 
the Petitioner, C. Tilaknandhan, regarding his unauthorized absence from duty since 27 May 2010, and that was 
acknowledged by the Petitioner. Exhibit M2 is the notice dated 247 November 2010 sent by Respondent bank to the 
Petitioner, C. Tilaknandhan, with reference to his unauthorized absence from duty. Further, it was also got received 
and acknowledged by the Petitioner. The second and final notice, Exhibit M3, was sent on 247 December 2010 by the 
Respondent, and it is mentioned therein that the Petitioner was advised once again to report for duty within 30 days 
from the date of the notice and explain the reason for his unauthorized absence and failing which, it will be deemed 
that he had voluntarily vacated his employment on expiry of the said period, as per clause (33) of the settlement of 
2005. This notice was also acknowledged by the Petitioner on 4"" January 2011. Document Exhibit M4 is the letter 
dated 24" January 2011, and it is mentioned there in that Petitioner has failed to report for duty therefore, he was 
deemed to have voluntarily vacated his employment from 24" January 2011, as per (33) of the bipartite settlement 
dated 2™ June 2005 and circular dated 17" June 2005. The order of competent authority in the matter of voluntary 
cessation of employment by C. Tilaknandhan, cashier clerk Murali, was annexed with this letter whereby the 
authority has passed the order that the Petitioner employee is deemed to have voluntarily vacated his employment 
from 24" January 2011, as per circular dated 17" June 2005. 


The order dated 24.01.2011 passed by the Respondent Authority in respect of employee deemed to have 
voluntary vacated has employment from 24.01.2011 is extracted here under: - 


“Sri C. Tilakanadan, Cashier Clerk, Moulali branch has been unauthorized absenting from duly. 
Therefore, he was advised vide Notice I vide letter dated 24.11.2010 to report for duty within 30 days from the 
date of the notice. The employee failed to report for duty within the said period. Thereafter, Notice (Final 
Notice) vide letter dated 24.12.2010 was served on the employee calling upon him to report for duty within 30 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 721 


days from the date of the said notice. 


The above letters were sent to the last known address furnished by the employee to the Bank. 
Considering the provisions of the Bipartite Settlement, it is deemed that they are duly served on the said 
employee. 


Further, records indicate that no prior permission has been obtained from the Competent Authority 
for his absence as his absence exceeds more than 90 days, he has committed breach of Clause 33 of Eighth 
Bipartite Settlement and despite giving further opportunity as stated above, he failed to respond and report for 
duty. Therefore, it is clear that he has no intention to report for duty and continue in service. 


In view of the foregoing, the employee is deemed to have voluntarily vacated his employment from 
24.01.2011 as per Clause 33 of Eighth Bipartile Settlement dated the 2nd June 2005, circulated vide 
H.O.Cir.No. PER/2005-06/16 dated the 17th June 2005 and it is ordered that he has voluntarily 
vacated the service.” 


12. Further, exhibit M6 is the circular dated 31st October 2006, regarding staff award unauthorized absence 
voluntary cessation from service and Clause (10) of this Exhibit 6 provides as extracted here under: - 


“The procedure in respect of the award staff employees working in Head Office Departments is as 
under 


If an employee working in a Departmental exceeds unauthorized absence of 90 days or more, the facts 
of the case should immediately be informed to chief Manager, Officer Administration Department, who shall 
undertake to serve first Notice (annexure-I) Second Notice (annexure-II), Order or of the competent Authority 
(annexure-IV) and Covering Letter (annexure-III) on the delinquent employee as per the procedure given 
under paras 5 to 7 above.” 


Thus, MWI has established that order dated 24.01.2011 has been passed by Competent Authority as per 
provision of Circular dated 31.10.2006, in respect of Petitioner deemed to have vacated his employment w.e.f 
24.01.2011. 


Respondent witness MW1 was also cross-examined at length by the Petitioner but nothing has been elicited 
to make the testimony of witness unbelievable. Rather, statement in chief of MW1 funds corroborated. Thus, in view 
of the above and ongoing the evidence on record Respondent has passed impugned order Ex: M4 according to rules 
and procedure as contain in circular dated 31.10.2006, and there is no illegality and infirmity in the order which 
warrant inference by this court. 


13. In this context the reference of decision of Hon’ble Supreme Court of India is relevant where in Apex Court 
has laid down the principle in the matter of termination of employer on the grounds of absenteeism: - 


(1) State of U.P. V. Ashok Kumar Singh 1996 (1) SCC 302, wherein the Apex Court had held: - 


"Having notices the fact that the first respondent has absented himself from duty without level on 
several not amount to such a grave charge Even otherwise on the facts of this case, there was no justification 
occasions, we are unable to appreciate the High Court's observation that his absence from duty would for the 
High Court to interfere with the punishment holding that the punishment does notice Tribunal with the gravity 
of the charge' especially when the High Court concurred with the findings of the Tribunal on facts. No case for 
interference with the punishment is made out.” 


(2) Similarly, in the case of North Eastern Karnataka R.T. Corpn. v. Ashappa decided on 12 May, 2006 
wherein, the Apex Court had held:- 


"Remaining absent for a long time, in our opinion, cannot be said to be a minor misconduct. The 
Appellant runs a fleet of buses. It is a statutory organization. It has to provide public utility services. For 
running the buses, the service of the conductor is imperative. No employer running a fleet of buses can allow 
an employee to remain absent for a long time. The Respondent has been given opportunities to resume his 
duties. Despite such notices, he remained absent. He wo found not only to have remained absent for a period of 
more than three years, but his leave record were also seen and it was found that he remained unauthorizedly 
absent on several occasions. In the view of the matter, it cannot be said that the misconduct committed by the 
Respondent herein h to be treated lightly." 


In another case, Delhi Transport Corporation v. Sardar Singh [(2004) 7 SCC 57 the Apex Court held: 


"11. Conclusions regarding negligence and lack of interest can be arrived at by looking into period of 
absence, more particularly, when same is unauthorized. Burden is on the employ who claims that there was no 
negligence and/or lack of interest to establish it by placing relevant materials. Clause (ii) of para 4 of the 
Standing Orders shows the seriousness attached habitual absence. In clause (i) thereof, there is requirement of 
prior permission. Only exception made is in case of sudden illness. There also conditions are stipulated, non- 


722 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


observance of renders the absence unauthorized." 


14. Therefore, in view of the foregone discussion, I am of the view that the action of the Respondent 
Management in issuing the proceeding dated 24.01.2011 through Ex.M4 informing the Petitioner he has failed to 
report for duty and he has voluntarily vacated his employment with effect from 24.01.2011, as per clause 33 of Eight 
Bipartite settlement dated 02-06-2005 and circular dated 17-06-2005, is legal and justified. Thus, Issue is decided 
against the Petitioner. 


ISSUE No. II: - 


In view of the finding and discussion given at Issue No. I, the action of Respondent management in passing 
the order dated 24.01.2011, as per clause 9 of the circular dated 31“ October 2006, of State Bank of Hyderabad issued 
by personnel department though which Petitioner has voluntarily vacated the services is legal and justified. Therefore, 
Petitioner is not entitled to any relief and petition filed by petition sans merit, deserves to be dismissed. 


Award 


The action of Respondent Management vide proceeding dated 247 January 2011. Informing the Petitioner 
that employee is deemed to have voluntarily vacated his employment from 24.01.2011, as per clause 33 of VII 
bipartite settlement dated 02.06.2005, circulated vide H.O Circular No. PER/2006-2007/60 dated 17.06.2005 and 
order that Petitioner has voluntarily vacated the office is legal and justified. Petition sans merit stand dismissed. 
Award is passed accordingly. 


Dictated to Smt. P. Phani Gowri, Personal Assistant, transcribed by her, corrected, and signed by me on this 
the 11" day of December, 2025. 


IRFAN QAMAR, Presiding Officer

Petitioner Respondent

(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 57 day of January, 2026) 


724 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


As per letter dated 15.02.2021 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification No. J- 
1(1-3)/2021-IR dt. 15.02.2021. The dispute under reference relates to: 


"क्या श्री नारायणदत शर्मा कर्मकार को Aas रेल अधिकारी क्लब FART 14/08/2014 को काम से निकाला जाना 
न्यायोचित है? यदि नहीं; तो उक्त कर्मकार को कब से ऑर किन लत्रा्भों के साथ Nest पर पुनः बहाल किया जाना 
चाहिए?" 


Case of the Workman is mainly that, he was first appointed as Caretaker on 01.07.2006 in the Narmada 
Railway Officers Club and has worked continuously with the Management till 14.08.2014 when his services were 
terminated without any notice or compensation which is in violation of Section 25F and 25G of the Act. He had put in 
08 years in continuous service and had thus acquired permanent status. The Management has thus adopted unfair 
labour practice in continuing him in service without granting him benefits of a permanent employee. He raised a 
dispute in this respect before the concerned Labour Commissioner, but after failure of conciliation this reference was 
made to this Tribunal. The Workman has thus prayed that, holding dismissal of his services unjust, illegal, improper 
and arbitrary he be held entitled to be reinstated with all back-wages and benefits. 


Case of the Management of Railway Club and Personnel Department West Central Railway has put their 
written statement of defense is that Western Railway has nothing to do with the Working of this Club as it is not a 
part of Railways in fact this is a club formed by Railway Officers to provide recreational facilities to its members who 
are Officers of Railways and their families. The Railway Club employs labour for its working. There is no relation of 
employer and employee between the Railways and the Workman. Also, it has been stated that, Railway Club itself is 
not an industry and also that workman never worked continuously for 240 days in any year. They have prayed that 
reference be answered against the Workman. 


In evidence, the Workman has filed his affidavit as his examination in chief. He has been cross-examined by 
Management. The Management of West Central Railway has field affidavit of its witness as his examination in chief. 
He has also been cross-examined by the Workman side. 


The bylaws of the Railway Club have been filed 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel Mr. Swapnil Khare for the workman and Mr. S.K. Gupta for the 
Management. I have gone through the record as well. 


The reference itself is the issue for determination in the case in hand. 


In his evidence, in form of Affidavit, the Workman has reiterated his allegations in the statement of claim as 
stated above. In his cross-examination, he admits that no advertisement for the post was released. No appointment 
letter was issued. No examination or test was conducted for appointment. Only he was assured to get his Casual Card, 
which was never issued. He Also stated that, his duty was from 7.00 AM to 10.00 P.M. and 05.00 P.M. to 09.00 A.M. 
and also that his attendance was taken in the Attendance Register of the Club. The Officers used to attend the Club for 
recreation and parties as well playing games. 


On the other hand, the Management witness has corroborated the pleadings by the side of Management in 
his affidavit, stating that Railway Club has nothing to do with Railways, rather it is a separate Club constituted by 
Railway Officers who are its members. The purpose of this Club is for providing recreational facilities to Officers and 
their families. Also, he stated that wages are paid by the Railway Club and not by Railways and that the workman did 
not worked for 240 days continuously in any year. 


In his cross-examination, the witness has stated that this Club is a Registered Organization, the workman 
who worked as a care-taker but was not on regular basis. 


The Bye-Laws of Railway Club are on record shows the purpose of the Club. According to Bye-Laws, the 
duty of the Club is to provide facilities of recreation and gatherings to their families. The Railway Officers, who are in 
service or retired, will be its members. There are also provisions for Management of this Club. 


Except the affidavit of the workman there is nothing on record in form of any appointment slip, wage slip or 
any other document to show that he was engaged by Railways. Hence, the relationship between the Workman and 
Railways with regard to employee and employer status is held not established. The Workman is held to have been 
engaged by the Railways Club, as admitted by the Workman himself. His work was for few hours in morning and 
evening in the Club. 


Section 2s of the Act is being reproduced as follows: 


(s) “workman” means any person (including an apprentice) employed in any industry to do any manual, 
unskilled, skilled, technical, operational, clerical or supervisory work for hire or reward, whether the terms of 
employment be express or implied, and for the purposes of any proceeding under this Act in relation to an 
industrial dispute, includes any such person who has been dismissed, discharged or retrenched in connection with, 
or as a consequence of, that dispute, or whose dismissal, discharge or retrenchment has led to that dispute, but 
does not include any such person— 


[भाग त--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 725 


(i) who is subject to the Air Force Act, 1950 (45 of 1950), or the Army Act, 1950 (46 of 1950), or the Navy 
Act, 1957 (62 of 1957); or 

(ii) who is employed in the police service or as an officer or other employee of a prison; or 

(iii) who is employed mainly in a managerial or administrative capacity; or (iv) who, being employed in 6 

supervisory capacity, draws wages exceeding 


ten thousand rupees per mensem or exercises, either by the nature of the duties attached to the office or by reason 
of the powers vested in him, functions mainly of a managerial nature. 


In the light of above discussion, holding the claim of the Workman not proved. The reference deserves 
to be answered against him. 


AWARD 


Holding the action of Management of M/s. Rail Adhikari Club in terminating the services of Mr. 
Narayandutt Sharma from 14.08.2014 is legal and justified in law. The workman is held entitled to no relief. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 05/01/2026 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer

(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 247 day of December - 2025) 


In Case No. R/85/2021, 


As per letter dated 28.12.2021 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification No. J- 
1(1-11)/2021-IR dt. 28.12.2021. The dispute under reference relates to: 


"क्या आवेवक श्री विनोव अहिरवार को दिनांक 01.08.2020 & बैंक ऑफ sar, शाया HRT Ree एवं 
विनांक27.05.2015 425.02.2020 तक बैंक ऑफ इंडिया; शाखा एयरपोर्ट रोड़ भोपान्र में प्यून/साफः TOS के प्रद पर 
कार्य करने उपरांत नॉकरी में स्थायी न किया जाना उचित है? ale नहीं तो आवेबक किस अनुतोष को पाने का 
हकवार हैं?" 


In RC/33/2022 the petition was filed by the workman against his same termination on the same facts and 


[भाग त--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 729 


grounds seeking same relief. 


In both the cases, the Management Bank took a case that, the Workman was never appointed against any 
regular vacancy following recruitment process. He never worked for 240 days in any year. Hence, his termination was 
not in violation of the Act. 


Since, the parties, the nature of disputes and the facts regarding the disputes was one and same, these two 
cases are being decided by a common judgment and order. 


During the proceedings, the workman died. His legal representatives did not filed any application to get 
them substituted in his place. No evidence was filed by the Workman. Management also filed no evidence. 


None was present for the workman at the time of argument. I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for 
the Bank Mr. A.B. Sahu and have gone through the record. 


The initial burden to prove his case is on the workman. By not filing any evidence he is held to have failed 
in discharging this burden. Hence, holding his case and claim not proved, the petitions deserves to be answered 
accordingly. 


AWARD 


Holding the action of Management of Bank of India in disengaging the Workman Vinod Ahirwar is held 
justified in law. His legal representatives are held entitled to no relief. 


A copy of this judgment and Award be kept on both the files. 
DATE:- 24/12/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer

का.आ. 125.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 


केनरा बैंक के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
औद्योगिक अधिकरण / श्रम न्यायालय चेन्नई के पंचाट (52/2025) wera करती है।

Award 


The Central Government, Ministry of Labour & Employment vide its Order No. 7/17/ 2024/RM/ 300102558/ 
ADJ/A1 dated 20.08.2025 referred the following Industrial Dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication. 


The schedule mentioned in that order is: 


“Whether the action of the Management of Canara Bank (E-Syndicate Bank) in issuing cessation 
proceedings to Ms. M. Panimalar, SWO “A” w.e.f. 20.08.2022 without conducting any inquiry is legal and 
justified? If not, to what relief is the concerned workman entitled to?” 


On receipt of the above reference dated 20.08.2025 from the appropriate Government, the dispute was 
registered as ID No. 52/2025, and due notices were issued to both the parties for their appearance. 


ID called out. Learned Counsel for the Petitioner and Respondent are present. Learned Counsel for the 
petitioner has filed a memo stating that the Industrial Dispute was instituted by the office of this Hon’ble Tribunal 
based on the failure report referred by the Regional Labour Commissioner (Central) at Madurai. Based on the same, 
the petitioner also filed an Industrial Dispute under Section 2A, which was numbered as ID No. 48/2025 and is 
pending before this Hon’ble Tribunal. It is therefore prayed that this Hon’ble Tribunal may be pleased to close 
ID No. 52/2025 on the file of this Tribunal. 


Hence the Industrial Dispute is hereby closed. 
An Award is passed accordingly.

सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण श्रम न्यायालय जबलपुर के पंचाट (15/2025) wera करती है। 


[a. एल-39025/01 /2026-आई.आर. (बी-1)-04]

their workmen. 


[भाग [ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 731 


[No. 1-39025/01/2026- IR (8-1)-04]

66 Rajaji Salai Through MD & CEO Indian Bank 
66 Rajaji Salai Chennai, 
Madras Tamil Nadu Pin No. 600001 
Respondent/Management 
AWARD 
(Passed on this 2201 day of December, 2025) 


The applicant workman has filed this petition u/s 2-A (2&3) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 as 
amended by Amendment Act of 2010 (in short ‘The Act’) now the Industrial Relation Code, 2020 (in short the 
‘Code’) against termination of his service by management Bank with a case that he was first appointed by Allahabad 
Bank on 01.09.2015 as a daily wager. He was falsely implicated in a criminal case relating to robbery of Bank money 
but was acquitted after trial. He was disengaged by management without any notice or compensation or enquiry 
which is against the Act and is illegal. He has prayed for holding this action of management Bank unjust, illegal and 
arbitrary, he be reinstated with all back wages and benefits. 


It is also the case of petitioner that during course of time Allahabad Bank merged with Indian Bank. 


Notices were sent to the management Bank but none appeared for them inspite of notices, hence the case 


732 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


proceeded ex-parte against the management Bank. 


In evidence, the petitioner filed his affidavit as his examination-in-chief, certificate with respect to failure of 
conciliation issued by Regional Labour Commissioner (Central), also filed copy of statement of witness Pradeep 
Nema in the concerned Session Trial No. 48/2022, State v/s Sunil Pandey & Ors and judgment of Court of Additional 
Session Judge, Durg in S.T. No. 48/2022. 


I have heard ex-parte argument from the side of workman. Workman has filed written submission also and I 
have gone through the record as well. 


In his uncontroverted affidavit, the petitioner has stated that he was first appointed in Allahabad Bank as a 
daily wager on 01.09.2015. The Deputy General Manager was informed by the Branch Manager with respect to his 
employment as daily wager, his name was also included in the Security Audit of the Bank conducted by police on 
18.01.2019, his name is mentioned as daily wager in this Audit Report also Branch Manager has recommended his 
regularization to the Deputy General Manager, Allahabad Bank vide his letter dated 17.06.2018. Further he has been 
issued two commendation letters on 04.04.2020 and 23.09.2020, for his good work during Covid period, his name 
was also included in the booklet of the Branch as a daily wager on 19.06.2020, he continued as such even after merger 
of Allahabad Bank with Indian Bank. He was falsely implicated in a police case as mentioned above and was 
acquitted after trial. He was discontinued and was not reinstated by the Bank after he approached them after his 
acquittal, he had completed 240 days in every year. 


This affidavit is uncontroverted. Petitioner has referred to judgment of Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case 
of H.D. Singh v/s Reserve Bank of India, (1986) AIR SC 132, in this case it has been held that striking off name of 
the worker from the attendance sheet as retrenchment as defined under section 2(0)(0) of the Act. 


Since, the claim of the petitioner that he worked 240 days in every year and that no notice or compensation 
was issued to him is uncontroverted, hence his disengagement by management Bank is held in violation of the Act. 


Though, the workman was acquitted after trial but only this fact does not entitle him to be reinstated but 
since he was disengaged without notice or compensation, a lump sum compensation of Rs. 1,00,000/- (One Lac Only) 
in lieu of all his claims will meet the ends of justice in my view to which he is held entitled to within 60 days from 
date of Award, failing which interest @ 6% per annum from the date of Award till payment. 


The reference stands answered accordingly. 
DATE: 22-12-2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer

(JUDGMENT) 


(Passed on this 167 day of December, 2025) 


As per letter dated 14/12/2015 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per 
Notification No.L-12011/69/2015 (IR(B-1)) dt. 14/12/2015. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“क्या दैनिक वेतनभोगी बैंक कर्मचारी संगठन FANT, बैंक ऑफ़ राजस्थान के आईसीआईसीआई IH त्रिमिटेड में 
वित्रय होने के पश्चात बैंक ऑफ राजस्थान TART ब्रायू किये गए9 वें विपक्षीय समझाँते के अनुसार अंशकालिक 
कर्मचारियों को विये जा रहे वेतन का ATH, आईसीआईसीआई बेंक त्रिमिटेड के ठेकेवारों CANT TATA बैंक; बैंक ऑफ 
राजस्थान के उन अशकालिक कर्मकारों के लिये जो वर्तमान में आईसीआईसीआई Fe त्रिमिटेड में ठेकेवारों दवारा 
कार्यरत हैं. पर MT रहेगा या TCT यवि हाँ तो वे कर्मकार किस अनुतोष के अधिकारी हैं? ' 


The case of the workman is mainly that, they are entitled to one-third of Scale wage admissible to 
subordinate staff which they were entitled before the merger of the Bank of Rajasthan with L-C.I.-C.I. Bank even after 
merger. It is further alleged that before merger, they were working as part time employees with the Bank of Rajasthan 
and were getting one-third of Scale wages which they were entitled to under 9" bipartite settlement. The Bank of 
Rajasthan merged with the L.C.I.C.I. Bank and at present they are working as employee of Contractor with the 
LC.LC.I. Bank but they are not being given one-third of the Scale wage for part time subordinate staff which they 
were getting before the merger which is unjust, illegal and arbitrary on the part of I.C.L.C.I. Bank. They have claimed 
that they be held entitled to one-third of the Scale wage admissible to subordinate staff in 9" bipartite settlement from 
the Contractors of I.C.LC.I. Bank. 


Case of management of I-C.I-C.I. Bank is mainly that the Bank of Rajasthan merged with LC.I.C.I. Bank 
vide order of Reserve Bank of India dated 12.08.2010 under the scheme of amalgamation which came into force from 
12.08.2010, the services of all regular employees of Bank of Rajasthan were transferred to the I.C.LC.I. Bank. The 
work of cleaning and sweeping is taken in the I.C.I.C.I. Bank by outsourcing agency at present outsourcing agency 
M/s Iris Services is providing these services through its employees. It is also their case that they were not signatory to 
the 9" bipartite settlement, hence it is not binding upon them. 


Photocopy documents have been filed and proved by the Union which are Ex-W/1 to W/11, to be referred to 
as and when require. 

The workmen Union has not filed any affidavit. Management has filed affidavit of its witnesses, the 
outsourced representative has also been examined. 

None appeared on behalf of the workmen Union at the stage of argument. Heard arguments of Learned 
Counsel Mr. Neeraj Kewat, no written submissions were filed. I have gone through the record as well. 

The reference itself is the issue for determination, in the case in hand. 


On perusal of 97 bipartite settlement reveals, the I.C.L.C.I. Bank is not a signatory to it, there is nothing in 
the merger document with respect to payment of wages to the part time employees of the transferor Bank i.e. Bank of 
Rajasthan on the basis of bipartite settlement after they merge with the I.C.LC.I. Bank, hence this claim of the 
workmen Union has no foundation of law. The claim is held not justified in law. 


734 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Accordingly, the reference is answered as follows. 
AWARD 


“Holding that the 9" bipartite settlement is not applicable to the ICICI Bank with respect to claims 
regarding scale wages which were applicable on them when they were working as daily wage employees of Bank of 
Rajasthan before merger with ICICI Bank, such workmen are held not entitled to any relief in this respect.” 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 16-12-2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer

का.आ. 128.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार 
सिंडिकेट बैंक, केनरा बैंक के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद 
में केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण / श्रम न्यायालय जबलपुर के पंचाट (06/2022) प्रकात करती है।

(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 187 day of December - 2025) 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 735 


As per letter dated 24.01.2022 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification 
No. J-1(1-16)/2021-IR dt. 24.01.2022. The dispute under reference relates to: 


क्या श्री anor fee Tenis, कर्मकार को Aad सिडीकेट dae / केनरा बैंक cant आवेवक को काम से निकाला जाना 
न्यायोचित हैं? यवि नहीं; तो उक्त कर्मकार को कब से और किन त्रा्ों के साथ नौकरी पर पुनः बहात्र किया जाना 
चाहिए?” 


The case of the Workman is mainly that, he was appointed by the then Syndicate Bank (merged with 
Canara Bank during the course of time) in June, 2012 on vacant and permanent post of sweeper after he was found 
suitable on the basis of the selection process but was treated as daily wager employee and was posted in the T.T. 
Nagar Branch of the Bank in Bhopal late he was transferred to Currency Chest Arera Colony Branch of the Bank in 
Bhopal where he worked till the date of his disengagement vide oral order dated 12.03.2020 without any notice or 
compensation and without any charge. He had worked continuously with the Bank as sweeper and had completed 240 
days in every year. The employees who joined later and were junior to him were retained and he was disengaged, 
hence, this action of Management Bank is in violation of Section 25F and 25G as well Rule 76 and 77 of Industrial 
Disputes Rules, 1957, he has requested that holding his termination against law, he be reinstated with back-wages and 
benefits and be also granted permanent status. 


Case of Management is mainly that, they have specific rules and regulations with respect to appointment of 
their employees, the post of Sweeper cum Peon on which Workman claims to have been engaged comes under the 
category of subordinate staff. He was never appointed by Bank following any recruitment procedure against any 
sanctioned vacancy. He might have worked as daily wager casual labour on intermittent basis which does not entitle 
him to continue his service or to have a permanent status. The Bank has accordingly requested that the reference be 
answered against the Workman. 


In evidence the Workman has filed and proved his Exhibit W-1 photocopy of his statement of Account from 
12.06.2012 to 12.03.2020. He has also filed his affidavit on which there is no cross-examination from the Bank. 
Management side has not filed any evidence in form of document or affidavit. 


I have heard arguments of Learned Counsel for the Workman Mr. Arun Patel and Mr. Jaideep Bansal 
Learned Counsel for the Bank. Management Bank has filed written submission also which are part of record. I have 
gone through the written submissions and the record. 


The reference is the issue for determination. 


Case of the Workman has been detailed earlier in his affidavit as his examination in chief, he has 
corroborated his case, and has stated that he was first appointed following recruitment process on the permanent post 
of Sweeper and worked with syndicate Bank continuously as a Daily wage employee. Earlier he was paid on daily 
basis in form of various charges through his account but from June, 2012 he was paid his wages on monthly basis 
through his Saving Account No. 77052010002294/- till March, 2020. His services were terminated under oral orders 
without any notice or compensation. He has further stated that he has worked for 240 days in every year and that after 
his disengagement he is unemployed. 


In absence of any cross-examination, this uncontroverted affidavit as examination-in-chief is worth reliance. 
This is further corroborated by statement of Account Exhibit W-1 which shows that he has received payments on 
monthly basis since 2010 a stated by him. 


On the other hand, there no evidence from the side of Bank that support their pleadings. 


Hence, from the evidence on record, the continuous employment of the Workman for 240 days as defined 
under Section 25B of the Act in every year including the year preceding the date of his termination is held proved. 
Since, there is nothing on record to show that any notice or compensation was given to him and a workman has 
specifically denied that he was issued any notice or compensation on his disengagement, termination of his services is 
held in violation of Section 25F and 25G of the Act. 


As regards relief, since there is nothing on record in form of appointment letter to show that the Workman 
was appointed following recruitment process against regular vacancy. As admitted by him, he was a daily wage 
employee, hence his reinstatement will not serve the ends of justice. 


In the light of facts and circumstances of the Case in hand, a lump sum compensation of Rs. 5,00,000/- 
(Rupees Five Lacs) payable to the Workman within 60 days from the date of Award, failing which interest @6% 
per annum from the date of Award in lieu of all his rights will meet the ends of justice to which he is held entitled. 


Reference is answered as follows. 
AWARD 
Holding the action of Management of M/s Syndicate Bank/ Canara Bank in terminating the services of 


736 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


the Workman Toran Singh Pathord illegal, unjust and arbitrary, the Workman is held entitled to a lump sum 
compensation of Rs. 5,00,000/- (Rupees Five Lacs) payable to him within 60 days from the date of Award, failing 
which interest @6% per annum from the date of Award in lieu of all his rights. 


DATE:- 18/12/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer

सिंडिकेट / केनरा बैंक के प्रबंधतत्र, संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में

(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 187 day of December - 2025) 


As per letter dated 24.01.2022 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification 
No. J-1(1-15)/2021-IR dt. 24.01.2022. The dispute under reference relates to: 


[भाग --खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 2026/माध 18, 1947 737 


"क्या श्री आशीष झा; कर्मकार को Aged सिडीकेट se / केनरा बैंक cant adage को काम से निकाला जाना 
न्यायोचित हैं? यदि नहीं; तो उक्त कर्मकार को कब से और किन ब्राभों के साथ नौकरी पर पुनः बहाल किया जाना 
चाहिए?” 


The case of the Workman is mainly that, he was appointed by the then Syndicate Bank (merged with 
Canara Bank during the course of time) in July, 2008 on vacant and permanent post of sweeper after he was found 
suitable on the basis of the selection process but was treated as daily wage employee and was posted in the Currency 
Chest of the Bank in Bhopal where he worked till 2012. He was further transferred to Indrapuri Branch and worked 
till date of his disengagement vide oral order dated 03.02.2020 without any notice or compensation and without any 
charge. He had worked continuously with the Bank as sweeper and had completed 240 days in every year. The 
employees who joined later and were junior to him were retained and he was disengaged, hence this action of 
Management Bank is in violation of Section 25F and 25G as well Rule 76 and 77 of Industrial Disputes Rules, 1957. 
He has requested that holding his termination against law, he be held reinstated with back-wages and benefits and be 
also granted permanent status. 


Case of Management is mainly that, they have specific rules and regulations with respect to appointment of 
their employees. The post of Sweeper cum Peon on which Workman claims to have been engaged comes under the 
category of subordinate staff. He was never appointed by Bank following any recruitment process against any 
sanctioned vacancy. He might have worked as daily wage casual labour on intermittent basis which does not entitle 
him to continue his service or to have a permanent status. The Bank has accordingly requested that the reference be 
answered against the Workman. 


In evidence, the Workman has filed and proved his Exhibit W-1, photocopy of his statement of Account 
from 01.07.2008 to 01.09.2019. He has also filed his affidavit on which there is no cross-examination from the 
Management Bank. The Management side has not filed any evidence in form of document or affidavit. 


I have heard arguments of Learned Counsel for the Workman Mr. Arun Patel and Mr. Jaideep Bansal 
Learned Counsel for the Bank. Management Bank has filed written submission also, which are part of record. I have 
gone through the written submissions and the record. 


The reference itself is the issue for determination. 


Case of the Workman has been detailed earlier in his affidavit as his examination in chief, he has 
corroborated his case and has stated that he was first appointed following recruitment process on the permanent post 
of Sweeper and worked with syndicate Bank continuously as a Daily wage employee. Earlier he was paid on daily 
basis in form of various charges through his account but from November, 2010 he was paid his wages on monthly 
basis through his Saving Account No. 77002010084923/- till June, 2020. His services were terminated under oral 
orders without any notice or compensation. He has further stated that he has worked for 240 days in every year and 
that after his disengagement he is unemployed. 


In absence of any cross-examination, this uncontroverted affidavit as examination-in-chief is worth reliance. 
This is further corroborated by statement of Account Exhibit W-1 which shows that he has received payments on 
monthly basis since 2010 as stated by him. 


On the other hand, there no evidence from the side of Bank that support their pleadings. 


Hence, from the evidence on record, the continuous employment of the Workman for 240 days as defined 
under Section 25B of the ‘Act’ in every year including the year preceding the date of his termination is held proved. 
Since, there is nothing on record to show that any notice or compensation was given to him and a workman has 
specifically denied that he was issued any notice or compensation on his disengagement, termination of his services is 
held in violation of Section 25F and 25G of the Act. 


As regards relief, since there is nothing on record in form of appointment letter to show that the Workman 
was appointed following recruitment process against regular vacancy as admitted by him, he was a daily wager, hence 
his reinstatement will not serve the ends of justice. 


In the light of facts and circumstances of the Case in hand, a lump sum compensation of Rs. 5,00,000/- 
(Rupees Five Lacs) payable to the Workman within 60 days from the date of Award, failing which interest @6% 
per annum from the date of Award in lieu of all his rights will meet the ends of justice to which he is held entitled. 


Reference is answered as follows. 
AWARD 
Holding the action of Management of M/s Syndicate Bank/ Canara Bank in terminating the services of 
the Workman Ashish Jha illegal, unjust and arbitrary, the Workman is held entitled to a lump sum compensation 


of Rs. 5,00,000/- (Rupees Five Lacs) payable to him within 60 days from the date of Award, failing which interest 
@6% per annum from the date of Award in lieu of all his rights. 


738 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 7, 2026/MAGHA 18, 1947 [Part II—SEC. 3(ii)]
